514	  }
515	  function showCheckpointList() {
516	    listShown = true;
517	    timerRunning = false;            // the run is complete — NOW the clock stops
518	    countdownEl?.classList.remove("is-low", "is-critical");
519	    if (termList) {
520	      termList.innerHTML = artifacts.map((a) =>
521	        `<div class="term-list-row"><span class="tl-id">` +
522	        `<span class="tl-key">Entire-Checkpoint:</span> ${a.id}</span>` +
523	        `<span class="tl-title">recovered: ${a.fragment.title}</span></div>`
524	      ).join("");
525	    }
526	    flashTerminal(`${artifacts.length} checkpoints linked · ship memory restored`, true);
527	    showTutorial("Memory restored — the ship knows the way home. Press Esc to close the terminal, then B to return to orbit.", 0);
528	    renderTerminal();
529	    onComplete?.();                  // Level 1 cleared — unlocks Level 2 in orbit
530	  }
531	
532	  // ---------- state transitions ----------
533	  function runStep(a) {
534	    if (a.state === "dormant") stage(a);
535	    else if (a.state === "recovered") commit(a);
536	  }
537	  function stage(a) {
538	    a.state = "recovered";
539	    teachOnce("staged",
540	      "Recovered and staged. Run `git commit` to freeze it — the clock is ticking.");
541	  }
542	  function commit(a) {
543	    a.state = "frozen";
544	    setModelOpacity(a.model, 1);
545	    const ice = makeIceBlock();
546	    ice.position.y = a.model.position.y + 0.6;
547	    a.iceY = ice.position.y;
548	    a.anchor.add(ice);
549	    a.ice = ice;
550	    teachOnce("committed",
551	      "Committed and frozen — locked in. Now Entire offers to link a checkpoint: press y to capture the session behind this commit.");
552	  }
553	  function confirmCheckpoint(yes) {
554	    const a = termTarget;
555	    if (!a || a.state !== "frozen") return;
556	    if (!yes) {
557	      flashTerminal("declined — without a checkpoint the ship can't track this commit. re-approach to link it.", false);
558	      closeTerminal(true);
559	      return;
560	    }
561	    checkpoint(a);
562	    if (allLinked()) {
563	      reviewMode = true;             // clock KEEPS running — the list is the finish line
564	      shipMeter?.classList.add("is-full");
565	      showTutorial("All banked! Now type `entire checkpoint list` before the clock hits 0:00 — hurry!", 0);
566	      renderTerminal();              // stay open → review step
567	    } else {
568	      closeTerminal(false);
569	    }
570	  }
571	  function checkpoint(a) {
572	    a.state = "checkpointed";
573	    a.id = genCheckpointId();
574	    const shipPct = 62 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 23); // 62–84%
575	
576	    if (a.ice) {
577	      a.ice.material.emissive.setHex(0x2a6c8a);
578	      a.ice.material.emissiveIntensity = 1.1;
579	      const core = new THREE.PointLight(0x8fe3ff, 6, 26, 2);
580	      core.position.copy(a.ice.position);
581	      a.anchor.add(core);
582	      a.core = core;
583	    }
584	    const spr = makeIdSprite(a.id);
585	    spr.position.y = a.iceY + 5;
586	    a.anchor.add(spr);
587	    a.idSprite = spr;
588	
589	    showCard(a, shipPct);
590	    setPower();
591	    teachOnce("checkpointed",
592	      "Checkpoint linked — Entire stamps it onto the commit via an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer, and the ship remembers a little more.");
593	  }
594	
595	  // ---------- input ----------
596	  function onCanvasClick() {
597	    if (!started) return;                 // briefing up — ignore world clicks
598	    if (active && !fp.isLocked) fp.lock();
599	  }
600	  function onKeyDown(e) {
601	    if (!active) return;
602	
603	    // Landing briefing is up — Enter/Space begins the run, nothing else.
604	    if (!started) {
605	      if (e.code === "Enter" || e.code === "Space") { startLevel(); e.preventDefault(); }
606	      return;
607	    }
608	
609	    // The run failed — only R (retry) does anything.
610	    if (failed) {
611	      if (e.code === "KeyR") { resetLevel(); e.preventDefault(); }
612	      return;
613	    }
614	
615	    // While the terminal is open, all keys feed it.
616	    if (terminalOpen) {
617	      if (e.code === "Escape") {
618	        // No bailing on the final review — the clock is still ticking.
619	        if (reviewMode && !listShown) { e.preventDefault(); return; }
620	        closeTerminal(true); e.preventDefault(); return;
621	      }
622	      if (reviewMode && listShown) { e.preventDefault(); return; } // only Esc closes the list
623	
624	      const step = currentStep();
625	      if (step?.type === "confirm") {
626	        if (e.key === "y" || e.key === "Y") { confirmCheckpoint(true); e.preventDefault(); return; }
627	        if (e.key === "n" || e.key === "N") { confirmCheckpoint(false); e.preventDefault(); return; }
628	        e.preventDefault();
629	        return;
630	      }
631	      // command mode
632	      if (e.code === "Enter") { submitCommand(); e.preventDefault(); return; }
633	      if (e.code === "Backspace") { buffer = buffer.slice(0, -1); renderTerminal(); e.preventDefault(); return; }
634	      if (e.key && e.key.length === 1 && !e.ctrlKey && !e.metaKey && !e.altKey) {
635	        buffer += e.key;
636	        renderTerminal();
637	        e.preventDefault();
638	      }
639	      return;
640	    }
641	
642	    // Walking around (terminal closed).
643	    if (e.code === "Escape" && fp.isLocked) fp.unlock();
644	    if (e.code === "KeyB") onExit?.();
645	  }
646	  fp.controls.addEventListener("lock", () => crosshair?.classList.remove("hidden"));
647	  fp.controls.addEventListener("unlock", () => crosshair?.classList.add("hidden"));
648	
649	  // ---------- HUD per-frame ----------
650	  function refreshHud() {
651	    if (!started || terminalOpen) {     // briefing up or terminal open → no walk HUD
652	      setPrompt(null);
653	      hideControls();
654	      hideActionBar();
655	      return;
656	    }
657	    setControls(fp.isLocked);
658	    hideActionBar();
659	    if (devLevel2) {
660	      setPrompt(fp.isLocked ? "Level 2 dev start — press B to return to orbit" : "Click to look around");
661	      return;
662	    }
663	    if (!fp.isLocked) { setPrompt("Click to look around"); return; }
664	    if (listShown) { setPrompt("Memory restored — press B to return to orbit"); return; }
665	    setPrompt("Find the surfacing memory");
666	  }
667	
668	  // ---------- per-frame ----------
669	  function update(dt, t) {
670	    fp.update(dt);
671	
672	    // Level countdown — one clock for the whole run.
673	    if (active && timerRunning && !failed) {
674	      timeLeft = Math.max(0, timeLeft - dt);
675	      updateClock();
676	      if (timeLeft <= 0) failLevel();
677	    }
678	    applyPanicSky();   // the sky reacts to the clock every frame
679	
680	    // Nearest memory within reach (ignoring height).
681	    let nearest = null, nd = Infinity;
682	    for (const a of artifacts) {
683	      const dx = camera.position.x - a.anchor.position.x;
684	      const dz = camera.position.z - a.anchor.position.z;
685	      const d = Math.hypot(dx, dz);
686	      if (d < nd) { nd = d; nearest = a; }
687	    }
688	    target = nd <= INTERACT_DIST ? nearest : null;
689	
690	    // Auto open/close the ship's terminal as you reach a memory.
691	    // (In review mode the terminal is driven manually, so leave it alone.)
692	    if (active && started && !reviewMode && !failed && !devLevel2) {
693	      if (dismissedMemory && target !== dismissedMemory) dismissedMemory = null;
694	      const canOpen = target && target.state !== "checkpointed" && target !== dismissedMemory;
695	      if (canOpen && !terminalOpen) openTerminal(target);
696	      else if (!canOpen && terminalOpen) closeTerminal(false);
697	    }
698	
699	    for (const a of artifacts) {
700	      // Melting away after a time-out — sink and fade, then disappear.
701	      if (a.melting) {
702	        a.meltT = Math.max(0, a.meltT - dt);
703	        const k = a.meltT / MELT_DUR;
704	        setModelOpacity(a.model, k);
705	        a.model.position.y -= dt * 2.4;
706	        if (a.ice) a.ice.material.opacity = 0.62 * k;
707	        a.beam.visible = false;
708	        a.glow.visible = false;
709	        continue;
710	      }
711	
712	      const encased = a.state === "frozen" || a.state === "checkpointed";
713	
714	      // Idle spin/bob until the memory is encased in ice.
715	      if (!encased) {
716	        a.model.rotation.y += dt * 0.6;
717	        a.model.position.y = 1.4 + Math.sin((t + a.bobOff) * 1.5) * 0.4;
718	        setModelOpacity(a.model, 1);
719	      }
720	
721	      // Beam + glow: bright while findable, gone once frozen.
722	      const lit = a.state === "dormant" || a.state === "recovered";
723	      let o = 0;
724	      if (a.state === "dormant") o = 0.4 + Math.sin(t * 2) * 0.12;
725	      else if (a.state === "recovered") o = 0.22;
726	      a.beam.material.opacity = o;
727	      a.beam.visible = o > 0.01;
728	      a.glow.visible = lit;
729	    }
730	
731	    if (terminalOpen) renderTerminal(); // reflect fade-driven state changes
732	    if (active) refreshHud();
733	  }
734	
735	  // ---------- lifecycle ----------
736	  function enter() {
737	    active = true;
738	    fp.groundAt(0, terrain.radius * 0.75);
739	    camera.lookAt(0, terrain.heightAt(0, 0) + 6, 0);
740	    canvas.addEventListener("click", onCanvasClick);
741	    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKeyDown);
742	    islandHud?.classList.remove("hidden");
743	    islandHud?.classList.toggle("is-level2-dev", devLevel2);
744	    devLevelBadge?.classList.toggle("hidden", !devLevel2);
745	    fpShared?.classList.remove("hidden");
746	    setControls(false);
747	    crosshair?.classList.add("hidden");
748	    setPower();
749	    if (devLevel2) {
750	      seedLevel2DevState();
751	      fp.attach();
752	      showTutorial("Level 2 dev mode — Level 1 checkpoints are preloaded.", 0);
753	    } else if (!started) {          // fresh landing — read the briefing, then START
754	      showBriefing();
755	    } else if (listShown) {         // truly done — the list has been run
756	      fp.attach();
757	      timerRunning = false;
758	      showTutorial("Memory restored — press B to return to orbit.", 0);
759	    } else if (failed) {
760	      resetLevel();                 // came back after a wipe → fresh run
761	    } else {
762	      fp.attach();
763	      timerRunning = true;          // resume the level clock
764	      if (reviewMode) {             // resume the timed final review
765	        openTerminal(artifacts[0]); // termTarget unused in review; reopens the prompt
766	        showTutorial("Type `entire checkpoint list` before the clock hits 0:00!", 0);
767	      } else {
768	        showTutorial("Memories are surfacing — the clock's short. Bank all three before 0:00!", 6500);
769	      }
770	    }
771	    updateClock();
772	    applyPanicSky();   // start on the right sky (calm unless we resumed low on time)
773	  }
774	  function exit() {
775	    active = false;
776	    timerRunning = false;           // pause the clock while in orbit
777	    closeTerminal(false);
778	    fp.unlock();
779	    fp.detach();
780	    canvas.removeEventListener("click", onCanvasClick);
781	    window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKeyDown);
782	    setPrompt(null);
783	    hideControls();
784	    hideActionBar();
785	    tutorialEl?.classList.add("hidden");
786	    ckptCard?.classList.add("hidden");
787	    termEl?.classList.add("hidden");
788	    levelFail?.classList.add("hidden");
789	    briefingEl?.classList.add("hidden");
790	    islandHud?.classList.add("hidden");
791	    islandHud?.classList.remove("is-level2-dev");
792	    devLevelBadge?.classList.add("hidden");
793	    fpShared?.classList.add("hidden");
794	    crosshair?.classList.add("hidden");
795	  }
796	  function resize() {
797	    camera.aspect = window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight;
798	    camera.updateProjectionMatrix();
799	  }
800	
801	  return { scene, camera, update, enter, exit, resize };
802	}
803

-50%) scale(0.96);\n  width: min(380px, 88vw);\n  padding: 20px 22px;\n  background: rgba(10, 8, 20, 0.92);\n  border: 1px solid rgba(255, 210, 122, 0.45);\n  border-radius: 10px;\n  box-shadow: 0   "new_string": "/* Hover (or keyboard focus, or synced from the 3D region) lifts the pin. */\n#island-pin:hover,\n#island-pin:focus-visible,\n#island-pin.is-hot {\n  transform: translate(-50%, -100%) scale(1.12);\n  filter:\n    drop-shadow(0 3px 5px var(--pin-shadow))\n    drop-shadow(0 0 12px rgba(255, 184, 107, 0.62));\n  outline: none;\n}\n\n/* After Level 1, the island's signal corrupts — the pin glitches magenta. */\n#island-pin.is-corrupted .pin-svg circle { fill: #ff2bd6; }\n#island-pin.is-corrupted .pin-svg path { stroke: rgba(255, 138, 216, 0.9); }\n#island-pin.is-corrupted {\n  filter:\n    drop-shadow(0 2px 4px var(--pin-shadow))\n    drop-shadow(0 0 10px rgba(255, 43, 214, 0.7));\n  animation: pin-glitch 2.2s steps(2, end) infinite;\n}\n@keyframes pin-glitch {\n  0%, 88% { transform: translate(-50%, -100%); }\n  90% { transform: translate(calc(-50% - 2px), -100%) skewX(6deg); }\n  94% { transform: translate(calc(-50% + 2px), -100%) skewX(-5deg); }\n  97%, 100% { transform: translate(-50%, -100%); }\n}"

114	
115	function switchTo(view) {
116	  if (transitioning || view === current) return;
117	  transitioning = true;
118	  fade.classList.add("show");
119	  setTimeout(() => {
120	    current.exit();
121	    current = view;
122	    current.enter();
123	    hint.classList.toggle("hidden", current !== planetView);
124	    fade.classList.remove("show");
125	    transitioning = false;
126	  }, 650); // match #fade CSS transition
127	}
128

133	  islandView.resize();
134	});

138	let firstFrame = true;
139	
140	function animate() {
141	  const dt = Math.min(clock.getDelta(), 0.05); // clamp big tab-switch jumps
142	  const t = clock.elapsedTime;
143	  current.update(dt, t);
144	  renderer.render(current.scene, current.camera);
145	
146	  if (firstFrame) {
147	    firstFrame = false;
148	    document.getElementById("loader").classList.add("done");
149	  }
150	  requestAnimationFrame(animate);
151	}
152	animate();

  "old_string": "const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);\nconst requestedView = params.get(\"view\");\nconst requestedLevel = params.get(\"level\");\nconst devStartLevel = requestedView === \"level2\" || requestedLevel === \"2\" ? 2 : 1;\n\n// ---------- views ----------\nconst planetView = createPlanetView(renderer, {\n  onIslandClick: () => switchTo(islandView),\n});\nconst islandView = createIslandView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n  devStartLevel,\n});\n\n// Default to the orbit view; ?view=island jumps to L1, while ?view=level2 or\n// ?level=2 jumps to the Level 2 development staging point.\nlet current = requestedView === \"island\" || devStartLevel === 2\n  ? islandView : planetView;\ncurrent.enter();\n\n// ---------- fade transition ----------\nconst fade = document.getElementById(\"fade\");\nconst hint = document.getElementById(\"hint\");\nlet transitioning = false;\nhint.classList.toggle(\"hidden\", current !== planetView);",
  "new_string": "const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);\nconst requestedView = params.get(\"view\");\nconst jumpToLevel2 = requestedView === \"level2\" || params.get(\"level\") === \"2\";\n\n// ---------- views ----------\n// After Level 1's `entire checkpoint list`, the island's signal corrupts:\n// the orbit pin glitches magenta and landing again enters Level 2.\nlet level1Done = jumpToLevel2;\n\nconst planetView = createPlanetView(renderer, {\n  onIslandClick: () => switchTo(level1Done ? corruptionView : islandView),\n});\nconst islandView = createIslandView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n  onComplete: () => { level1Done = true; },\n});\nconst corruptionView = createCorruptionView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n});\n\n// Default to the orbit view; ?view=island jumps straight to Level 1 and\n// ?view=level2 (or ?level=2) straight to Level 2 — handy for development.\nlet current = requestedView === \"island\" ? islandView\n  : jumpToLevel2 ? corruptionView : planetView;\ncurrent.enter();\n\n// ---------- fade transition ----------\nconst fade = document.getElementById(\"fade\");\nconst hint = document.getElementById(\"hint\");\nconst pin = document.getElementById(\"island-pin\");\nlet transitioning = false;\n\nfunction refreshOrbitHud() {\n  hint.classList.toggle(\"hidden\", current !== planetView);\n  hint.textContent = level1Done\n    ? \"The island signal is corrupting — click the pin to go back down\"\n    : \"Drag to orbit · Scroll to zoom · Click the pin to land\";\n  pin?.classList.toggle(\"is-corrupted\", level1Done);\n}\nrefreshOrbitHud();"
}

  "content": "import { chromium } from \"playwright\";\n\nconst URL = \"http://localhost:5174/?view=level2\";\nconst shot = (page, name) => page.screenshot({ path: `/tmp/l2-${name}.png` });\nconst sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));\n\nconst browser = await chromium.launch({\n  headless: true,\n  args: [\"--autoplay-policy=no-user-gesture-required\"],\n});\nconst page = await browser.newPage({ viewport: { width: 1280, height: 800 } });\npage.on(\"pageerror\", (e) => console.log(\"PAGEERROR:\", e.message));\npage.on(\"console\", (m) => { if (m.type() === \"error\") console.log(\"CONSOLE:\", m.text()); });\n\nawait page.goto(URL);\nawait sleep(2500);\n\n// 1. Briefing should be up\nconst briefingVisible = await page.evaluate(() =>\n  !document.getElementById(\"l2-briefing\").classList.contains(\"hidden\"));\nconsole.log(\"briefing visible:\", briefingVisible);\nconsole.log(\"briefing title:\", await page.textContent(\"#l2-briefing .bf-title\"));\nawait shot(page, \"1-briefing\");\n\n// 2. Dismiss with Enter\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");\nawait sleep(500);\nconsole.log(\"briefing dismissed:\", await page.evaluate(() =>\n  document.getElementById(\"l2-briefing\").classList.contains(\"hidden\")));\nconsole.log(\"corrupt meter:\", await page.textContent(\"#corrupt-pct\"));\nconsole.log(\"clue count:\", await page.textContent(\"#clue-count\"));\n\n// 3. Click canvas → pointer lock\nawait page.click(\"#scene\", { position: { x: 640, y: 400 } });\nawait sleep(600);\nconst locked = await page.evaluate(() => document.pointerLockElement !== null);\nconsole.log(\"pointer locked:\", locked);\n\nconst promptText = () => page.evaluate(() =>\n  document.getElementById(\"fp-prompt\").textContent);\nconst terminalOpen = () => page.evaluate(() =>\n  !document.getElementById(\"terminal\").classList.contains(\"hidden\"));\n\nconsole.log(\"prompt:\", await promptText());\n\n// 4. Walk forward toward the console (camera starts facing it)\nawait page.keyboard.down(\"ArrowUp\");\nlet opened = false;\nfor (let i = 0; i < 30; i++) {\n  await sleep(150);\n  if (await terminalOpen()) { opened = true; break; }\n}\nawait page.keyboard.up(\"ArrowUp\");\nconsole.log(\"terminal opened by walking:\", opened);\nawait shot(page, \"2-terminal\");\nconsole.log(\"term hint:\", await page.textContent(\"#term-hint\"));\nconsole.log(\"term cta:\", await page.textContent(\"#term-cta\"));\n\n// 5. Search before any clue — gating message\nawait page.keyboard.type('entire checkpoint search \"course plot\"');\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");\nawait sleep(300);\nconsole.log(\"search (no clues) list:\", await page.evaluate(() =>\n  document.getElementById(\"term-list\").innerText.replace(/\\n/g, \" | \")));\nconsole.log(\"search msg:\", await page.textContent(\"#term-msg\"));\nawait shot(page, \"3-search\");\n\n// 6. Explain the good candidate\nawait page.keyboard.type(\"entire checkpoint explain 8c21d4e7a90f\");\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");\nawait sleep(300);\nconsole.log(\"explain output:\", await page.evaluate(() =>\n  document.getElementById(\"term-list\").innerText.replace(/\\n/g, \" | \")));\nawait shot(page, \"4-explain\");\n\n// 7. Probe: bogus command + bogus explain id\nawait page.keyboard.type(\"entire checkpoint explain zzzz\");\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");\nawait sleep(200);\nconsole.log(\"bogus explain msg:\", await page.textContent(\"#term-msg\"));\nawait page.keyboard.type(\"git push --force\");\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");\nawait sleep(200);\nconsole.log(\"bogus cmd msg:\", await page.textContent(\"#term-msg\"));\n\n// 8. Rewind browser\nawait page.keyboard.type(\"entire checkpoint rewind\");\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");\nawait sleep(300);\nconsole.log(\"browser list:\", await page.evaluate(() =>\n  document.getElementById(\"term-list\").innerText.replace(/\\n/g, \" | \")));\nawait shot(page, \"5-rewind-browser\");\n\n// 9. Probe: rewind to the DECOY first (newest first → index 0 is 04:15 tainted)\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");      // select top (tainted)\nawait sleep(200);\nconsole.log(\"confirm line:\", await page.textContent(\"#term-input\"));\nconst pctBefore = await page.textContent(\"#corrupt-pct\");\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"y\");\nawait sleep(600);\nconsole.log(\"decoy rewind msg:\", await page.textContent(\"#term-msg\"));\nconsole.log(\"corruption surge:\", pctBefore, \"→\", await page.textContent(\"#corrupt-pct\"));\nawait shot(page, \"6-decoy-softfail\");\n\n// 10. Rewind again to the GOOD checkpoint (index 2: 03:12)\nawait page.keyboard.type(\"entire checkpoint rewind\");\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");\nawait sleep(300);\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"ArrowDown\");\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"ArrowDown\");\nawait sleep(200);\nconsole.log(\"selected row:\", await page.evaluate(() =>\n  document.querySelector(\".term-list-row.is-sel\")?.innerText.replace(/\\n/g, \" \")));\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"Enter\");\nawait sleep(200);\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"y\");\nawait sleep(1500);\nawait shot(page, \"7-restoring\");\nawait sleep(4500);   // restore animation\nconst winVisible = await page.evaluate(() =>\n  !document.getElementById(\"l2-win\").classList.contains(\"hidden\"));\nconsole.log(\"win screen visible:\", winVisible);\nconsole.log(\"win sub:\", await page.textContent(\"#l2-win-sub\"));\nawait shot(page, \"8-win\");\n\n// 11. B returns to orbit; pin should be corrupted\nawait page.keyboard.press(\"b\");\nawait sleep(1500);\nconsole.log(\"hint:\", await page.textContent(\"#hint\"));\nconsole.log(\"pin corrupted:\", await page.evaluate(() =>\n  document.getElementById(\"island-pin\").classList.contains(\"is-corrupted\")));\nawait shot(page, \"9-orbit\");\n\nawait browser.close();\nconsole.log(\"DONE\");\n"
}

> AGENT

Here's the back-to-basics pitch.

## Level 2: one new command — `entire checkpoint rewind`

**The whole idea:** Level 2 is the *same game the player already knows* — walk, `git add`, `git commit`, bank checkpoints, beat the clock. One thing is different: midway through the run, a scripted glitch storm hits and **melts everything you've banked**.

That's the moment. In Level 1, "MEMORIES MELTED" was the fail screen — permanent, run over, press R. So when the player watches their banked memories sink into the sand *mid-run*, they think they've lost. But the terminal lights up with one line:

> `they were checkpointed. nothing banked is ever lost.`
> TYPE `entire checkpoint rewind`

Type it, and time visibly runs backwards — the memories rise out of the ground, re-freeze, the ice glow comes back. Then you finish the level normally.

**Why this works:**
- **Zero new systems.** No clues, no search syntax, no IDs to compare, no browser UI. The player does exactly what L1 trained them to do, plus types one new command once.
- **The tension is already built.** Same countdown, same panic sky — and the storm *eats clock time*, so rewind saves your work but you still have to hustle to the finish.
- **It teaches the actual value proposition.** L1 taught "checkpoints bank your work." L2 teaches the punchline: "banked means you can always get it back." The melt-then-rewind beat is L1's worst fear, reversed by one command. That's the superpower moment with nothing else in the way.

**Shape of the run:** land → same briefing style, one new line ("storms have been hitting the island") → collect and bank like L1 → at the moment you bank the 2nd memory, the storm hits and melts everything → terminal teaches `entire checkpoint rewind` → world rewinds → bank the last memory → `entire checkpoint list` finish line, same as L1.

One small design question before I build: when the storm melts the work, should the **clock keep ticking** during the melt-and-rewind beat (max tension, rewind feels urgent), or **pause for the teaching moment** and resume after (calmer, makes sure the lesson lands)? My lean is pause — it's the one moment in the level where we want the player reading, not panicking.

Also: I'll rip out the corruption implementation (`corruptionView.js` + its HTML/CSS) and build this on top of the existing `islandView` code instead, since it reuses nearly everything. Want me to go ahead?

> DEVELOPER

dont make any changes yet. what is the point of the rewind if it's being used in this way?

> AGENT

Honest answer: in the way I pitched it, rewind is mis-modeled — and your question exposes exactly the flaw.

**What I pitched:** an external disaster (storm) destroys your banked work, and rewind restores it. That's not rewind — that's *restore from backup*. It quietly teaches a wrong mental model in two directions:

1. **It implies checkpoints can be lost.** In the real tool, the whole point is the opposite: checkpoints are the safe, durable thing. Nothing external threatens them. A game beat where banked checkpoints melt actually undermines the trust the tool is trying to build.
2. **It hides what rewind actually trades.** Real `entire checkpoint rewind` takes your *current working state* back to a past checkpoint. The thing at risk is never the bank — it's the work you've done *since* the last checkpoint. Rewind says: "forward progress has gone bad; give it up and return to the last known-good moment." You sacrifice the bad recent work to recover the good past.

So the real point of rewind, in one line: **checkpoints are save points, and rewind is reloading your save.** It exists because you (or your AI agent) sometimes drive the current state into a ditch — a bad change, a wrong direction — and the cheapest fix isn't repairing forward, it's going back. The value it demonstrates isn't "your bank is safe from storms," it's "you can take risks, because any mistake after a checkpoint is reversible."

**What that implies for the level, if we keep it minimal:** the disaster shouldn't come from outside — it should come from *forward progress going wrong*. Something like: the player banks two memories (safe), then the third one they handle goes bad — committing it corrupts the ship's current state, meter drains, world glitches. Rewind returns the world to the moment of the last checkpoint: the bad commit is gone, the two banked memories are untouched. Then they finish properly. Same single new command, same reuse of L1, but now the fiction matches the tool: the bank was never in danger, only the work since the last save — and rewind is how you get back to it.

No changes made — that's the assessment. Want me to shape that corrected version into the actual Level 2 beats?

> DEVELOPER

i dont like the idea of using rewind. let's look at some other commands for ideas or even some skills..we have entire search, recap, resume, dispatch..that we can explore..dont implement anything

> AGENT

Good — these are actually richer veins than rewind. Quick grounding from the real CLI, then a minimal game shape for each. (Fun note: your original `debris.js` fragments already seeded this — the helmet hints `recap`, the beacon hints `dispatch`, the panel hints `activity`. Past-you was planning a command per artifact.)

## What each command is actually *for*, and its smallest game shape

**`entire recap` — "what was I doing?"**
Real value: you come back after time away and recap rebuilds your context from checkpoint activity — you don't re-read everything, the tool catches you up.
Game shape: Level 2 opens with *you* having the amnesia this time. You land after a blackout — island dark, fog everywhere, beams out, and you genuinely can't see what's left to do. The terminal offers one command: `entire recap`. Typing it replays your Level 1 run — your actual checkpoint IDs scroll past, "here's what you banked, here's what's still missing" — and as it prints, the fog lifts and the remaining memories light up. Then it's the collect-and-bank loop you already know, under the clock.
Load: one command, zero new mechanics. The demonstration is built into the drama: darkness → recap → the world makes sense again. And it mirrors the game's own theme — the cure for lost context.

**`entire dispatch` — "tell the others what happened"**
Real value: composes a summary of recent agent work so you can hand it off — the status update writes itself.
Game shape: the finish line changes. The ship can't call for rescue until it can *say what happened*. After banking everything, instead of `checkpoint list`, you type `entire dispatch` — and the game composes the transmission *from your actual run* (which memories, what order, how much clock was left) and fires it into the sky as the win moment. The value beat is watching a summary you didn't write be accurate about what you did.
Load: one command, replaces the existing finish line. Slightly weaker mid-level tension (it's an ending beat, not a mechanic), but the strongest narrative payoff — dispatch is literally the call home.

**`entire checkpoint search` — "find that moment"**
Real value: your history is queryable; you don't have to remember where anything is.
Game shape: only convincing at scale — searching the 3 things you banked is a shrug. You'd need a fiction like "the previous crew's log has 40 checkpoints" so the list overwhelms and search cuts through. That means adding a corpus *and* a reason to search (a clue or target), which is exactly the variable-creep you flagged on the corruption idea. I'd shelve it for a later level.

**`entire session resume` — "pick up where a session left off"**
Real value: sessions persist; work-in-progress isn't lost between sittings (or between people).
Game shape: a half-recovered memory on the island — someone started it and stopped. `entire session resume` and you inherit their progress mid-stream. Neat, but it requires teaching *sessions* as a concept distinct from checkpoints — a new noun, which is real cognitive load. Also better later.

**Skills (`entire labs review` / `investigate`)** — experimental in the real CLI and multi-concept. Endgame material, not Level 2.

## My take

**Recap is the Level 2 command.** It's the only one where the value demo *is* the gameplay opener rather than a new system: one typed command turns confusion into orientation, then the level is pure L1 muscle memory with the existing tension. And it sets up a clean arc — L1: bank it. L2: come back and recap it. L3: dispatch it home — with search/resume/rewind available as deeper levels if the game grows.

Nothing implemented — including the corruption code from earlier still sitting uncommitted in the working tree, which I'll strip out whenever you've settled on a direction. What's your read on recap?

> DEVELOPER

okay but what would we do that warrants needing a recap..so far level 1 we just collected 3 items.

> AGENT

Right — nothing in Level 1 warrants a recap. Three items, all fully banked, finished seconds ago: any player can hold that in their head. If recap just reprints it, it reads as ceremony, not value.

Recap earns its keep under three conditions, and Level 2 would have to manufacture them:

1. **More state than your head holds.** Not 3 memories — 6 or 7.
2. **Mixed states, not just done/not-done.** The L1 loop already has in-between states: a memory can be *staged*, or *committed but never checkpointed* (they pressed n, or walked away). With 6 memories mid-run, you naturally end up with "2 banked, 1 frozen but unbanked, 3 untouched" — and *that's* what's genuinely hard to track. The real product value of recap is exactly this: not "what's finished" but "what was in flight."
3. **An interruption that hides the visible state.** Here's the honest trick: the reason players don't need recap is that the *island shows them everything* — glowing ice means banked, beams mean untouched. So the interruption has to wipe the display. A blackout hits mid-run: screen cuts, you wake back at the spawn point, and every artifact looks the same — dark, frosted, unreadable. Now the player's actual memory is the only record of which of 6 things was in which state... and it fails, because the blackout was scripted to land at the most chaotic moment.

Then `entire recap` is the cure: it prints the session state — banked ✓, frozen-but-not-checkpointed ⚠ ("finish this one first"), untouched ✗ — and as it prints, the beams relight color-coded. Orientation restored, finish the run.

One mechanic detail that makes the teaching land without forcing it: after the blackout, the player *can* recover without recap — by walking to all six artifacts and checking them one by one. The clock makes that ruinously expensive. Recap is instant. So the command isn't a gate, it's the obviously rational move — which is the actual pitch of the tool: not "you must," but "this saves you the walk."

So the level in one line: **same loop, doubled scale, a blackout that wipes the world's memory at the worst moment, and one new command that gives it back.** The recap has something real to say precisely because the work was bigger, messier, and interrupted.

Still not touching code. Does this version of "warranting a recap" feel right, or does doubling the item count already smell like variable-creep to you?

> DEVELOPER

maybe entire search would fit a little better..let's explore that

> AGENT

Search is a good fit — arguably better than recap, because its value can be demonstrated *repeatedly* as a mechanic instead of once as a cutscene. The key is what search is actually for: **you remember a word, not a place.** It turns a fuzzy memory of content into an exact location in your history. So the level has to make "finding by walking" hopeless and "finding by word" instant.

## Level 2: "The Archive"

**Fiction:** Level 1's win restored the ship's memory — and the side effect is that the ship's *entire pre-crash archive* surfaces. The island is now dotted with twenty-odd frozen memories: identical ice blocks, no beams, no labels readable from a distance. Everything the previous voyage ever checkpointed. The ship needs three specific ones to plot the way home, and it tells you in plain language:

> "I need the memory of the **ignition sequence**."

**The loop (×3):**
1. The ship asks for a memory, describing it in a sentence — the search word is right there in the request.
2. Walking the field and inspecting blocks one by one is possible — and ruinous under the clock. Twenty identical blocks, one countdown.
3. At the terminal: `entire checkpoint search "ignition"` → the matching checkpoint prints, **and its beam lights up across the island**. That's the money shot, three times: a dark field, one typed word, one light.
4. Sprint to it, press E, memory transmits to the ship, power meter climbs. Next request.

Mistyped or wrong word? Zero results, no penalty except the ticking clock — which is exactly the real-world cost of bad search terms.

**Why this fits your constraints:**
- **One new command**, typed three times — repetition is how the syntax sticks.
- **Reuses L1 entirely:** same island, same terminal, same beams/clock/meter. The only inversion: in L1 the game lit the beams for you; in L2 *nothing lights up until you search*. Search literally turns the lights on.
- **No variable-creep.** Deliberately absent: no clue-hunting (the quest sentence contains the word), no decoy results, no timestamps, no `explain`, no browser UI, no new artifact states. The 20 ice blocks are set dressing, not state to track.
- **The value is felt, not told.** The contrast between "I could check 20 blocks" and "I typed one word" *is* the lesson, and the clock makes you feel it.

And it sharpens the arc: **L1 — bank your memories. L2 — your history is bigger than your head; search it.** Recap/dispatch/resume stay on the shelf for later levels.

One open design choice: when you reach the found block, is retrieval just **E → transmitted** (keeps L2's typing focused purely on the search command — my lean), or a mini version of L1's terminal beat to keep the muscle memory warm? Still not touching code until you call it.

> DEVELOPER

are you saying that ship asks for a specific thing..player types a keyword..the item lights up..and the player runs over and grabs it??

> AGENT

Yes — exactly that. Four beats, repeated three times:

1. **Ship asks:** "I need the memory of the *ignition sequence*."
2. **Player types:** `entire checkpoint search "ignition"`
3. **The match lights up** — one beam switches on somewhere out in a field of identical dark ice blocks.
4. **Player runs over and grabs it** (E), it transmits to the ship, meter climbs, next request.

The clock runs the whole time, so the tension is sprint-shaped, like Level 1. And the teaching is in the contrast you feel at step 2-3: twenty unreadable blocks out there, one typed word finds the right one instantly.

That's the entire level. No other new mechanics.

> DEVELOPER

okay and for level 2..we can have multiple items in our checkpoint list that we didnt grab in level 1 . that's totally okay..just so we can have more options.

> AGENT

Exactly — that's the move that makes search worth teaching. The archive is the ship's full history, not just your three: dozens of checkpoints from the previous voyage that the player never personally banked. Fiction-wise it's clean (Level 1 restored ship memory → the whole archive surfaced), and it's honest to the real tool — your checkpoint history quickly grows past what you can remember, *which is the very thing search exists for*. Your three L1 checkpoints can still appear in the list as a nice continuity touch, but the corpus is big enough that browsing is hopeless and searching is obvious.

1	# Planetfall — Plan & Status
2	
3	_Last updated: 2026-06-05_
4	
5	## The pitch
6	
7	You're a stranded astronaut. Your ship's AI has **amnesia** — it lost all its
8	context about what happened. To escape, you explore a 3D planet, find debris,
9	and each piece hides a small puzzle solved by **recovering lost context**.
10	
11	The hook: reconstructing the ship's memory in-fiction **is the same act** as
12	recovering a real codebase's history with [Entire](https://docs.entire.io).
13	Each puzzle teaches a real `entire` command.
14	
15	> Note: `entire explain` does **not** exist (it was an early guess). The real
16	> context-recovery commands are `recap`, `checkpoint`, `activity`, `dispatch`.
17	
18	## Locked decisions
19	
20	- **3D, web-based.** Three.js + Vite, no downloaded assets — the planet is
21	  generated procedurally at load.
22	- **Planet look (approved):** a dreamy **lavender ocean world** with a few
23	  **gold metallic islands** that shimmer, wrapped in a **Saturn-style ring**.
24	  Soft, minimal, calm.
25	  - NOT earthlike. NOT the glowing-magenta-"neural"-vein look (tried it; it
26	    looked "itchy" — busy land texture fighting bright veins. Removed.)
27	  - Keep lighting (sun / ambient / env) **low** — the lavender sea washes out
28	    white if over-lit.
29	- **Build order:** get the world feeling right **first** (done), then puzzles,
30	  then the command engine + win/lose.
31	- **Two-level structure (approved 2026-06-05):** the game is two views, not one.
32	  - **Orbit view** — bird's-eye planet; click a marked **island hotspot** to land.
33	  - **Island view** — a **walkable, first-person** landscape (WASD + mouse-look);
34	    artifacts are embedded **in the terrain**, not floating on the globe. Walk up
35	    to one and press **E** to inspect; **B** leaves back to orbit.
36	  - Transition is a **crossfade cut** into a dedicated island scene (not a
37	    seamless dive onto the sphere).
38	  - Starting with **one island holding all the artifacts/puzzles**; more later.
39	
40	## Status
41	
42	### Done ✅ — the 3D world (user-approved)
43	- Procedural planet: lavender ocean, gold **metallic** islands (PBR
44	  metalness + low roughness + `RoomEnvironment` IBL → shimmer), soft frost
45	  poles, drifting clouds.
46	- Saturn-style banded ring (tilted, two gaps, transparent).
47	- Soft lavender fresnel atmosphere, violet-tinted starfield.
48	- Orbit + zoom controls; slow auto-rotation.
49	- Verified rendering in headless Chrome (WebGL2), no console errors.
50	
51	### Done ✅ — the two-level flow (2026-06-05)
52	- **Orbit view** (`planetView.js`): the planet plus a clean **HTML map pin**
53	  ("LANDING SITE") that tracks the island in screen space — no glow/beam. On
54	  hover a thin **outline ring** fades in on the surface and the pin lifts; click
55	  (pin or island) → crossfade → island. The island is found via a shared
56	  elevation sampler, and the **camera frames it front-and-centre on load**. Pin
57	  hides when the island rotates to the far side.
58	- **Island view** (`islandView.js`): a procedural **walkable terrain** island
59	  (radial-falloff heightmap, gold highlands, surrounded by lavender sea),
60	  lavender-dusk sky, soft warm sun. **First-person** controller (`firstPerson.js`,
61	  PointerLockControls + WASD, glued to the terrain, clamped to the island).
62	- The 4 artifacts (reusing the `debris.js` prop builders) sit **in the terrain**
63	  with tall findable light-beams; walk within range → "Press E to inspect" →
64	  opens the shared fragment panel. **B** returns to orbit.
65	- Verified both views render in headless Chrome (WebGL2), no console errors.
66

76	
77	## Puzzle design (PROPOSED — not yet reviewed/built)
78	
79	Arc: **orient → restore → understand → escape**. Each puzzle is small: run a
80	command → read the recovered context → use one detail from it.
81	
82	| # | Fragment | Command | What's lost | Puzzle |
83	|---|----------|-----------|-------------|--------|
84	| 1 | 🪖 EVA Helmet | `recap` | "What was I doing before the crash?" | Run `recap`; read the summary of recent log entries; pick out the mission. |
85	| 2 | 📖 Torn Logbook | `checkpoint` | A setting got corrupted in the crash | Search checkpoints, find the last good one before the corruption, rewind to it. |
86	| 3 | 🖥️ Nav Panel | `activity` | "*When* did it go wrong?" | Read the activity timeline; find when the anomaly started. |
87	| 4 | 📡 Signal Beacon | `dispatch` | No way to call for help | Generate a dispatch summarizing all recovered context, broadcast it → **win**. |
88	
89	- **#4 is the ending**: you can only send a complete dispatch after recovering
90	  the other three. This quietly answers "how do you win / how do checkpoints
91	  accumulate."
92	
93	### Open questions (decide before/while building puzzles)
94	1. **Does the arc + mapping feel right?** (e.g. helmet → recap)
95	2. **Command engine:** simulated in-game terminal (portable, shareable web demo)
96	   vs. real CLI execution (authentic, needs a local backend) vs. hybrid.
97	   _Leaning simulated for a shareable demo; not yet decided._
98	3. **How literal is the command output?** Real-ish `entire` output vs. softened
99	   ship-AI flavor text.
100	4. **Win/lose model:** currently leaning "restore the ship's log" (collect all
101	   fragments, no hard lose state). Oxygen/timer and branching endings were
102	   other options.
103	
104	### Next step
105	Fully design **#1 (EVA Helmet / `recap`)** end-to-end as the template, then
106	clone the pattern to the other three.
107	
108	## Level 1 design — "First Memories" (agreed 2026-06-09)
109	
110	Framing inspiration: **Diner Dash**. The fun isn't the task, it's that every
111	action feeds a clear goal, each task is a short chain, a timer pushes you, and
112	you juggle toward a target. The three workflow beats map onto a Diner Dash
113	table:
114	
115	- **Do (recover/fix)** = take the order + cook
116	- **Freeze (commit)** = serve the food — locked in, but not scored yet
117	- **Checkpoint** = collect the check — *this is the step that banks progress*
118	
119	Key teaching point: in Diner Dash you can serve every table and score nothing if
120	you never collect. Same here — a frozen-but-not-checkpointed fix is a served
121	plate you never got paid for. **Freeze keeps it; checkpoint banks it.** The ship
122	rebuilds from checkpoints, not bare commits, so checkpoints literally ARE
123	forward progress.
124	
125	What a checkpoint shows (middle-ground detail — not the full real anatomy): a
126	real **12-char hex ID** (e.g. `711044b1fe29`, like real Entire checkpoints),
127	the commit **trailer** it's stamped with (`Entire-Checkpoint: <id>`), a
128	**one-line summary** of the fix, and a tiny
129	**who-did-what bar** (ship % / you %). Stands in for the real
130	transcript/prompts/token-usage/line-attribution without a spreadsheet. The ID
131	also appears stamped on the commit (ice block) so the link is visible.
132	
133	### Pressure: one level countdown (not per-memory)
134	A single **countdown clock** (top-left HUD, `TIME m:ss`) runs for the whole run
135	— tuned **scary-tight** (`TOTAL_TIME`, currently **45s** for 3 memories;
136	one-line tune in `islandView.js`). Bank every memory before it hits `0:00` or
137	the run **fails**: every un-banked memory **melts away** (sinks + fades, ice
138	included) and a "MEMORIES MELTED" screen offers **press R to try again** (full
139	reset). The clock escalates: **urgent** (red, pulsing) under 22s → **critical**
140	(frantic fast pulse + shake + glowing red digits) under 8s; it pauses while
141	you're in orbit. The **whole sky panics** too — starting at `PANIC_TIME` (22s)
142	the lavender dusk, fog, dome and sun lerp toward an angry **crimson**, eased so
143	it's barely perceptible at first and only fully reads in the last ~10s, with a
144	**throb** once critical (and held full-red behind the fail screen). One knob
145	(`PANIC_TIME` in `islandView.js`) moves the sky independently of the clock. The clock keeps running through the **final
146	`entire checkpoint list`** — that command is the real finish line, so banking
147	the last memory does NOT stop the clock; you still have to type the list before
148	`0:00` (and you can't Esc out of that final prompt). Time out there and the
149	whole run melts. This replaced the old per-memory ~14s fade — the user wanted
150	ONE clock and real pressure across the whole level, not individual items melting.
151	
152	### The loop (one memory at a time — calm tutorial, no juggling yet)
153	The player types the **real commands** into the ship's terminal (not abstract
154	keys). Walking up to a memory auto-opens an in-world command line (movement
155	freezes; keystrokes feed the terminal; Esc backs out; B returns to orbit).
156	Each step shows the exact command to type as a scaffold.
157	
158	1. A memory **surfaces** — light beam on the terrain.
159	2. Walk up → the **ship terminal opens** (`crashlog:~$`), hinting `git add`.
160	3. Type **`git add`** → memory is staged/recovered.
161	4. Type **`git commit`** → encased in **ice** (commit).
162	5. Entire then **offers to link a checkpoint** — `Link this commit to a
163	   checkpoint? [y/n]`. Press **`y`** → ice lights up, a floating real
164	   **12-char hex ID** (e.g. `711044b1fe29`) appears, the record card pops,
165	   **ship power ticks up**. (Decline with `n`
166	   and it stays an un-tracked commit — re-approach to link it later.)
167	6. Walk to the next memory.
168	7. When all are linked → **the clock is still ticking** → race to type
169	   **`entire checkpoint list`** before `0:00` to see every checkpoint you
170	   collected → ship fully restored (win). Miss it and the whole run melts.
171	
172	IMPORTANT accuracy note (user corrected this): you do NOT type `entire
173	checkpoint` to save — in real Entire, linking a checkpoint is a yes/no offer
174	made after a commit. `entire checkpoint list` is the real command, used at the
175	end to review what you've banked.
176	
177	~3 memories; tutorial scaffolds the first of each action, near-silent after.
178	Wrong commands get a "command not recognized — try: <cmd>" nudge.
179	
180	The make-it-click beats, taught via the countdown + the meter:
181	- the level **clock** is the pressure — anything not banked before `0:00` melts.
182	- `git commit` freezes it but **doesn't move the meter** — only linking the
183	  checkpoint (`y`) banks it. Teaches commit-vs-checkpoint, no "go back" mechanic.
184	
185	### Out of scope for L1 (later levels)
186	Juggling multiple memories / tighter timers; the "go back / reapply a checkpoint"
187	payoff; recap / activity / dispatch; win/lose beyond the power meter.
188	
189	## Architecture / where things live
190	
191	```
192	index.html         # canvas + HUD + crossfade overlay + FP prompt + panel + loader
193	src/style.css      # HUD / panel / loader / fade / crosshair / FP-prompt styling
194	src/main.js        # VIEW MANAGER: owns renderer + resize + shared panel + the
195	                   #   render loop; crossfades between the two views.
196	                   #   ?view=island jumps straight onto the island (dev aid).
197	src/planetView.js  # orbit view: scene/lighting/stars/planet/clouds/atmo/ring +
198	                   #   the clickable gold landing hotspot. onIslandClick callback.
199	src/islandView.js  # island level: sky/light, terrain+water, the artifacts, the
200	                   #   proximity "press E" interaction. enter()/exit()/onExit.
201	src/terrain.js     # procedural island heightmap mesh + water + heightAt(x,z)
202	src/firstPerson.js # PointerLockControls + WASD walker, glued to the terrain
203	src/noise.js       # seedable 3D simplex + fBm (sphere-sampled → seamless)
204	src/planet.js      # bakes planet maps; createElevationSampler() finds land for
205	                   #   the hotspot. Palette constants (DEEP/SHALLOW/SNOW) here.
206	src/atmosphere.js  # fresnel limb-glow shell shader (orbit view)
207	src/ring.js        # procedural banded ring (radial-remapped UVs)
208	src/debris.js      # FRAGMENTS[] (lore + command) + exported prop BUILDERS,
209	                   #   reused as the island artifacts. createDebris() is now
210	                   #   unused (kept for reference / git history).
211	```
212	
213	Run: `npm install` then `npm run dev` → http://localhost:5173
214	Controls: orbit = drag/scroll, click the gold marker to land. Island = click to
215	capture mouse, WASD to walk, E to inspect a glowing artifact, B to leave.
216	
217	## History
218	Repo started as `signalkit` (a Python telemetry CLI), then pivoted to Planetfall
219	on 2026-06-05. The old code is recoverable via git history + Entire checkpoints.
220	
```

  "new_string": "### Out of scope for L1 (later levels)\nJuggling multiple memories / tighter timers; the \"go back / reapply a checkpoint\"\npayoff; recap / activity / dispatch; win/lose beyond the power meter.\n\n## Level 2 design — \"The Archive\" (agreed 2026-06-09, NOT built yet)\n\n**Design rule (user, 2026-06-09): one new command per level, and it must\nvisibly show the value of checkpoints.** Level 2's command is\n**`entire checkpoint search`**. Nothing else is new.\n\nRejected on the way here (kept for the record so we don't re-tread):\n- An elaborate \"corruption\" level teaching search + explain + rewind with\n  clue-hunting, decoy checkpoints, and a spreading corruption zone — **too many\n  variables to consume at once**.\n- `rewind` as the L2 command — in the simple framings it kept mis-modeling the\n  tool (reading as \"restore from backup\" when real rewind means \"take my\n  *current* state back to a save point; the bank itself is never at risk\").\n- `recap` — L1's three collected items don't warrant a recap; manufacturing the\n  need (more items, mixed states, a blackout) was creeping back toward\n  variable-overload.\n\n### The fiction\nLevel 1's win restored the ship's memory — side effect: the ship's **entire\npre-crash checkpoint archive surfaces**. The island is now dotted with ~20\nidentical dark frozen memories (ice blocks, no beams, unreadable from a\ndistance). Most were never grabbed by the player — the corpus is deliberately\n**bigger than your head**, because that's the situation search exists for.\nThe player's 3 L1 checkpoints can appear in the archive as a continuity touch.\n\n### The loop (×3, one countdown clock for tension)\n1. **The ship asks** for a memory in a plain sentence that contains the\n   keyword: *\"I need the memory of the **ignition sequence**.\"* (No clue\n   hunting — the search word is right there in the request.)\n2. **Player types** `entire checkpoint search \"ignition\"` at the terminal.\n3. **The matching block's beam lights up** across the island — the money shot:\n   a dark field, one typed word, one light. (In L1 the game lit the beams for\n   you; in L2 nothing lights until YOU search.)\n4. **Sprint over, press E** → memory transmits to the ship, power meter\n   climbs, next request.\n\nWrong/misspelled word → 0 results, no penalty except the ticking clock (which\nis exactly the real-world cost of bad search terms). Searching by walking the\nfield and inspecting blocks one-by-one stays *possible* but ruinously slow —\nthe tool isn't a gate, it's the obviously rational move.\n\nThe teaching, felt not told: **your history quickly outgrows your memory;\nyou remember a *word*, not a place — search turns the word into the moment.**\nArc so far: L1 — bank it. L2 — search it. (recap / dispatch / resume / rewind\nreserved for later levels.)\n\n### Deliberately NOT in Level 2\nNo clue collection, no decoy results, no timestamps, no `explain`, no rewind\nbrowser, no new artifact states. The ~20 archive blocks are set dressing, not\nstate to track. Retrieval is a plain E-grab — the level's typing effort lives\nin the search command itself (leaning: keep it that simple rather than rerun\nL1's add/commit beat per item; revisit after a playtest).\n\n### Cleanup owed before building\nAn abandoned corruption-level implementation sits uncommitted in the working\ntree (`src/corruptionView.js`, `#l2-hud` markup in `index.html`, L2 styles in\n`style.css`, view wiring in `main.js`) — strip it. Keep from that work:\n`src/memoryProps.js` (shared ice/beam/ID-sprite props), the `onComplete` hook\nin `islandView.js`, the `#fp-shared` HUD split, and the corrupted-pin entry\nconcept (after L1 completes, the orbit pin glitches and landing again enters\nLevel 2; `?view=level2` / `?level=2` dev shortcuts).\n\n## Architecture / where things live"
}

Added to `PLAN.md` — there's now a **"Level 2 design — 'The Archive' (agreed 2026-06-09, NOT built yet)"** section right after the Level 1 design, written in the same style as the rest of the doc. It captures:

- **The design rule** you set: one new command per level, and it must show the value — L2's command is `entire checkpoint search`, nothing else new.
- **The rejected paths** (corruption multi-command level, rewind, recap) with one line each on *why*, so we don't re-tread them later.
- **The fiction**: L1's win surfaced the ship's full pre-crash archive — ~20 identical dark ice blocks, a corpus deliberately bigger than your head.
- **The loop ×3**: ship asks in a plain sentence containing the keyword → type `entire checkpoint search "ignition"` → the matching block's beam lights up → sprint over, E to grab. One countdown clock; bad searches cost only time.
- **What's deliberately excluded**: no clues, decoys, explain, timestamps, or new states; retrieval is a plain E-grab.
- **Cleanup owed before building**: strip the abandoned corruption implementation from the working tree, keeping the few reusable pieces (`memoryProps.js`, the `onComplete` hook, the shared HUD split, and the corrupted-pin entry into Level 2).

No game code touched. Whenever you're ready, the next step is that cleanup pass followed by building the Archive level.

7	// The walkable island level — LEVEL 1 ("First Memories").

62	function normalizeCmd(s) {
63	  return s.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, " ");
64	}
65	function cmdMatches(input, accepts) {
66	  const n = normalizeCmd(input);
67	  return accepts.some((a) => n === a || n.startsWith(a + " "));
68	}
