1	import * as THREE from "three";
2	import { makeNoise3D, makeFbm } from "./noise.js";
3	
4	// A walkable island: a displaced plane with a radial falloff so the land rises
5	// out of a lavender sea and tapers back into it at the edges. Palette matches
6	// the planet — gold highlands, pale-gold shore, lavender shallows.
7	
8	// Colors as 0..1 rgb triples (vertex colors are linear-ish; kept soft).
9	const C_DEEP = [0.27, 0.21, 0.46];    // lavender lowland near water
10	const C_SHORE = [0.78, 0.66, 0.42];   // pale gold sand
11	const C_GOLD = [0.86, 0.66, 0.27];    // gold midland
12	const C_PEAK = [0.96, 0.86, 0.62];    // bright gold / cream highland
13	
14	function mix(a, b, t) {
15	  return [a[0] + (b[0] - a[0]) * t, a[1] + (b[1] - a[1]) * t, a[2] + (b[2] - a[2]) * t];
16	}
17	function ramp(t) {
18	  if (t < 0.18) return mix(C_DEEP, C_SHORE, t / 0.18);
19	  if (t < 0.5) return mix(C_SHORE, C_GOLD, (t - 0.18) / 0.32);
20	  return mix(C_GOLD, C_PEAK, Math.min(1, (t - 0.5) / 0.5));
21	}
22	
23	/**
24	 * Build the island terrain.
25	 *
26	 * @param {object} opts
27	 * @param {number} opts.size     world units across (square)
28	 * @param {number} opts.segments grid resolution
29	 * @param {number} opts.maxHeight peak elevation in world units
30	 * @param {number} opts.seed
31	 * @returns {{ mesh, water, size, maxHeight, heightAt(x,z), radius }}
32	 */
33	export function createTerrain({
34	  size = 200,
35	  segments = 220,
36	  maxHeight = 26,
37	  seed = 1337,
38	} = {}) {
39	  const noise = makeNoise3D(seed);
40	  const fbm = makeFbm(noise, { octaves: 5, lacunarity: 2.1, gain: 0.5 });
41	
42	  const half = size / 2;
43	  const seaLevel = 0; // world-space y of the surrounding water plane
44	
45	  // Raw elevation field in world units for a given world (x,z). Combines fbm
46	  // detail with a smooth radial island mask (1 at centre → 0 past the radius).
47	  const islandRadius = half * 0.86;
48	  function rawHeight(x, z) {
49	    const d = Math.hypot(x, z) / islandRadius;
50	    // Smooth falloff: flat-ish plateau in the middle, sinks below sea at edge.
51	    const mask = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(1 - d * d, 0, 1);
52	    const n = fbm(x * 0.015, 0, z * 0.015) * 0.5 + 0.5; // 0..1, gentle hills
53	    const ridges = fbm(x * 0.05 + 100, 5, z * 0.05 - 100) * 0.5 + 0.5; // finer
54	    const e = (n * 0.7 + ridges * 0.3) * mask;
55	    // Push the shoreline down so the centre clearly stands above the water.
56	    return (e - 0.18) * maxHeight;
57	  }
58	
59	  // Bake the grid once into a Float32 height table for fast bilinear queries.
60	  const n = segments + 1;
61	  const step = size / segments;
62	  const table = new Float32Array(n * n);
63	  for (let j = 0; j < n; j++) {
64	    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
65	      const x = -half + i * step;
66	      const z = -half + j * step;
67	      table[j * n + i] = rawHeight(x, z);
68	    }
69	  }
70	
71	  // Bilinear height lookup in world space, clamped to the grid.
72	  function heightAt(x, z) {
73	    const fx = THREE.MathUtils.clamp((x + half) / step, 0, segments);
74	    const fz = THREE.MathUtils.clamp((z + half) / step, 0, segments);
75	    const i0 = Math.floor(fx), j0 = Math.floor(fz);
76	    const i1 = Math.min(i0 + 1, segments), j1 = Math.min(j0 + 1, segments);
77	    const tx = fx - i0, tz = fz - j0;
78	    const h00 = table[j0 * n + i0], h10 = table[j0 * n + i1];
79	    const h01 = table[j1 * n + i0], h11 = table[j1 * n + i1];
80	    const a = h00 + (h10 - h00) * tx;
81	    const b = h01 + (h11 - h01) * tx;
82	    return a + (b - a) * tz;
83	  }
84	
85	  // ---- mesh ----
86	  const geo = new THREE.PlaneGeometry(size, size, segments, segments);
87	  geo.rotateX(-Math.PI / 2); // lay flat on the XZ plane, +Y up
88	  const pos = geo.attributes.position;
89	  const colors = new Float32Array(pos.count * 3);
90	  for (let v = 0; v < pos.count; v++) {
91	    const x = pos.getX(v);
92	    const z = pos.getZ(v);
93	    const y = table[
94	      Math.round((z + half) / step) * n + Math.round((x + half) / step)
95	    ];
96	    pos.setY(v, y);
97	    // Color by height above sea; underwater fringe stays lavender.
98	    const t = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(y / maxHeight + 0.18, 0, 1);
99	    const c = ramp(t);
100	    colors[v * 3] = c[0]; colors[v * 3 + 1] = c[1]; colors[v * 3 + 2] = c[2];
101	  }
102	  geo.setAttribute("color", new THREE.BufferAttribute(colors, 3));
103	  geo.computeVertexNormals();
104	
105	  const mesh = new THREE.Mesh(
106	    geo,
107	    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({
108	      vertexColors: true,
109	      roughness: 0.85,
110	      metalness: 0.15, // a faint sheen so it still feels related to the gold world
111	      flatShading: false,
112	    })
113	  );
114	  mesh.receiveShadow = true;
115	
116	  // ---- surrounding water ----
117	  const water = new THREE.Mesh(
118	    new THREE.PlaneGeometry(size * 4, size * 4),
119	    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({
120	      color: 0x4a3a86,        // lavender sea, matching the planet's DEEP tone
121	      transparent: true,
122	      opacity: 0.86,
123	      roughness: 0.25,
124	      metalness: 0.0,
125	    })
126	  );
127	  water.rotation.x = -Math.PI / 2;
128	  water.position.y = seaLevel;
129	
130	  return { mesh, water, size, maxHeight, heightAt, radius: islandRadius, seaLevel };
131	}
132

  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\nimport { createTerrain } from \"./terrain.js\";\nimport { createFirstPerson } from \"./firstPerson.js\";\nimport { makeBeamTexture, makeIceBlock, makeIdSprite } from \"./memoryProps.js\";\n\n// LEVEL 2 (\"The Archive\") — the same island, revisited.\n//\n// Restoring the ship's memory in Level 1 surfaced its full pre-crash archive:\n// ~20 identical dark ice blocks, every checkpoint the old crew ever banked.\n// Far too many to read one by one — which is exactly the point. One new\n// command on top of the loop the player already knows:\n//\n//   ship asks for a memory (the keyword is in its request sentence)\n//   `entire checkpoint search \"<word>\"`  → the matching block's beam lights up\n//   sprint over, press E                 → the memory transmits to the ship\n//\n// Repeated ×3 under one countdown. Wrong or 0-result searches cost nothing\n// but clock time — that's the whole pressure model.\n\nconst INTERACT_DIST = 8;     // how close (XZ) to transmit a lit block (E)\nconst CONSOLE_DIST = 9;      // how close (XZ) to open the ship terminal\nconst TOTAL_TIME = 90;       // seconds to search & transmit all three memories\nconst LOW_TIME = 35;         // clock turns urgent (red, pulsing) under this\nconst CRIT_TIME = 12;        // clock goes CRITICAL (fast pulse) under this\nconst PANIC_TIME = 35;       // the SKY starts shifting toward panic-red under this\nconst SPRITE_DIST = 18;      // walk this close to a dark block to read its id\n\n// Sky panic palette — same island, same dread as Level 1's clock.\nconst SKY_CALM  = new THREE.Color(0x2a2350);\nconst SKY_PANIC = new THREE.Color(0x6e0f16);\nconst FOG_PANIC = new THREE.Color(0x4a0a0e);\nconst DOME_CALM = new THREE.Color(0x3a3168);\nconst DOME_PANIC = new THREE.Color(0x7a141c);\nconst SUN_CALM  = new THREE.Color(0xfff1dc);\nconst SUN_PANIC = new THREE.Color(0xff5a3c);\n\nconst CONSOLE_POS = { x: -6, z: 56 };\n\n// What the ship asks for, in order. Each request is a plain sentence that\n// CONTAINS the keyword — no clue hunting, just read and search.\nconst REQUESTS = [\n  {\n    targetId: \"9d4e1f7a02bc\",\n    line: \"Power is back, but the engines won't fire. Three entries answer the requests above;\n// the rest is everything else the old crew ever checkpointed (including the\n// three memories banked in Level 1). A corpus bigger than your head — search.\nconst ARCHIVE = [\n  { id: \"9d4e1f7a02bc\", summary: \"ignition timing tuned — engines certified for cold start\" },\n  { id: \"5b82c3d9e016\", summary: \"starmap charted — a route home through the lavender belt\" },\n  { id: \"c7f0a45d138e\", summary: \"long-range antenna aligned — relay handshake held steady\" },\n  { id: \"31f0cafe4d12\", summary: \"recovered: abandoned EVA helmet\" },\n  { id: \"7e11a2b09c44\", summary: \"recovered: torn logbook page\" },\n  { id: \"b0a7ded51a6e\", summary: \"recovered: cracked nav panel\" },\n  { id: \"2b90cc41d7ae\", summary: \"ballast trim balanced for the sea crossing\" },\n  { id: \"f7d3a2e85c10\", summary: \"hull seams resealed after the first squall\" },\n  { id: \"4c61e0b9d827\", summary: \"water recycler filters swapped and flushed\" },\n  { id: \"a93f57c12e08\", summary: \"cargo manifest reconciled against the hold\" },\n  { id: \"0e7b249fa6d3\", summary: \"greenhouse lamps recalibrated for the dark season\" },\n  { id: \"61d8f3a07b5c\", summary: \"airlock seals pressure-tested to spec\" },\n  { id: \"8a05b6e49c21\", summary: \"fuel cells rebalanced across bank three\" },\n  { id: \"d24c91f7e60a\", summary: \"landing struts torqued and pinned\" },\n  { id: \"3f6a08d5c4b9\", summary: \"thermal shielding patched on the leeward side\" },\n  { id: \"b15e7c20a9f4\", \"16a9d5e3b07c\", summary: \"archive index rebuilt overnight\" },\n];\n\nfunction normalizeCmd(s) {\n  return s.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\\s+/g, \" \");\n}\n\n// Deterministic RNG so the archive blocks land in the same spots every run.\nfunction mulberry32(seed) {\n  return function () {\n    seed |= 0; seed = (seed + 0x6d2b79f5) | 0;\n    let t = Math.imul(seed ^ (seed >>> 15), 1 | seed);\n    t = (t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), 61 | t)) ^ t;\n    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;\n  };\n}\n\n// The ship console the terminal lives in (same prop as the crash site).\nfunction makeConsole() {\n  const g = new THREE.Group();\n  const pedestal = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.BoxGeometry(2.6, 4.6, 1.6),\n    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x1c2230, metalness: 0.6, 0x2a8aa6, emissiveIntensity: 1.4, roughness: 0.3,\n    })\n  );\n  screen.position.set(0, 4.0, 0.82);\n  screen.rotation.x = -0.28;\n  g.add(pedestal, screen);\n  return g;\n}\n\nexport function createArchiveView(renderer, { onExit } = {}) {\n  const canvas = renderer.domElement;\n\n  // ---------- scene & sky (Level 1's calm lavender, healed) ----------\n  const scene = new THREE.Scene();\n  scene.background = SKY_CALM.clone();\n  scene.fog = new THREE.Fog(SKY_CALM.clone(), 90, 320);\n\n  const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(\n    62, window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight, 0.1, 90, 40);\n  scene.add(sun);\n  scene.add(new THREE.AmbientLight(0x6a5a92, 0.3));\n\n  const dome = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.SphereGeometry(900, 32, 16),\n    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: DOME_CALM.clone(), side: THREE.BackSide, fog: false })\n  );\n  scene.add(dome);\n\n  // ---------- terrain (the same island) ----------\n  const terrain = createTerrain({ size: 200, segments: 220, return; }\n      if (e.key && e.key.length === 1 && !e.ctrlKey && !e.metaKey && !e.altKey) {\n        buffer += e.key;\n        renderTerminal();\n        e.preventDefault();\n      }\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // Walking around.\n    if (e.code === \"KeyE\" && nearBlock && !won) { tryTransmit(nearBlock); return; }\n    if (won) { setPrompt(\"Archive linked — press B to return to orbit\"); return; }\n    if (nearBlock) {\n      if (nearBlock.grabbed) setPrompt(\"Already transmitted — the ship has this one\");\n      else if (nearBlock.lit) setPrompt(\"Press E — transmit to the ship\");\n      else setPrompt(\"A dark archive block — the terminal can find it by name\");\n      return;\n    }\n    if (blocks.some((b) => b.lit)) {\n      setPrompt(\"Follow the lit beam — press E at the block to transmit\");\n      return;\n    }\n    setPrompt(\"Read the ship's request, then search at the gold-beam terminal\");\n  }\n\n  // ---------- per-frame ----------\n  function update(dt, t) {\n    fp.update(dt);\n\n    // Level countdown — one clock for the whole run.\n    if (active && timerRunning && !failed && !won) {\n      timeLeft = Math.max(0, timeLeft - dt);\n      updateClock();\n      if (timeLeft <= 0) failLevel();\n    }\n    applyPanicSky();\n\n    // Lit beams pulse so they read as \"go here\" from across the island.\n    for (const b of blocks) {\n      if (b.lit) b.beam.material.opacity = 0.45 + Math.sin(t * 2.5) * 0.15;\n      // Ids stay anonymous from afar; reveal when lit, grabbed, or walked up to.\n      if (!b.grabbed && !b.lit) {\n        const d = Math.hypot(\n          camera.position.x - b.anchor.position.x,\n          camera.position.z - b.anchor.position.z\n        );\n        b.sprite.visible = d <= SPRITE_DIST;\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Proximity: nearest archive block, and the ship terminal.\n    nearBlock = null;\n    let nd = Infinity;\n    for (const b of blocks) {\n      const d = Math.hypot(\n        camera.position.x - b.anchor.position.x,\n        camera.position.z - b.anchor.position.z\n      );\n      if (d < nd) { nd = d; if (d <= INTERACT_DIST) nearBlock = b; }\n    }\n    nearConsole = Math.hypot(\n      camera.position.x - CONSOLE_POS.x, camera.position.z - CONSOLE_POS.z\n    ) <= CONSOLE_DIST;\n\n    // The terminal opens itself when you reach the console, like Level 1.\n    if (active && started && !failed && !won) {\n      if (consoleDismissed && !nearConsole) consoleDismissed = false;\n      if (nearConsole && !terminalOpen && !consoleDismissed) openTerminal();\n      else if (!nearConsole && terminalOpen) closeTerminal(false);\n    }\n\n    if (active) refreshHud();\n  }\n\n  // ---------- lifecycle ----------\n  function enter() {\n    active = true;\n    fp.groundAt(0, terrain.radius * 0.75);\n    camera.lookAt(CONSOLE_POS.x, terrain.heightAt(CONSOLE_POS.x, CONSOLE_POS.z) + 5, CONSOLE_POS.z);\n    canvas.addEventListener(\"click\", onCanvasClick);\n    window.addEventListener(\"keydown\", onKeyDown);\n    l2Hud?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    fpShared?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    setControls(false);\n    crosshair?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    updateRequest();\n    if (!started) {\n      showBriefing();\n    } else if (won) {\n      fp.attach();\n      winEl?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    } else if (failed) {\n      resetLevel();                 // came back after a wipe → fresh run\n    } else {\n      fp.attach();\n      timerRunning = true;          // resume the level clock\n      showTutorial(\"The ship is still waiting — read its request and search the archive.\", 6000);\n    }\n    updateClock();\n    applyPanicSky();\n  }\n  function exit() {\n    active = false;\n    timerRunning = false;           // pause the clock while in orbit\n    closeTerminal(false);\n    fp.unlock();\n    fp.detach();\n    canvas.removeEventListener(\"click\", onCanvasClick);\n    window.removeEventListener(\"keydown\", onKeyDown);\n    setPrompt(null);\n    hideControls();\n    tutorialEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    termEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    failEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    winEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    briefingEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    l2Hud?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    fpShared?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    crosshair?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n  }\n  function resize() {\n    camera.aspect = window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight;\n    camera.updateProjectionMatrix();\n  }\n\n  return { scene, camera, update, enter, exit, resize };\n}\n"

  "old_string": "// ---------- views ----------\n// After Level 1's `entire checkpoint list`, the island's signal corrupts:\n// the orbit pin glitches magenta and landing again enters Level 2.\nlet level1Done = jumpToLevel2;\n\nconst planetView = createPlanetView(renderer, {\n  onIslandClick: () => switchTo(level1Done ? corruptionView : islandView),\n});\nconst islandView = createIslandView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n  onComplete: () => { level1Done = true; },\n});\nconst corruptionView = createCorruptionView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n});",
  "new_string": "// ---------- views ----------\n// After Level 1's `entire checkpoint list`, the ship's full archive surfaces:\n// the orbit pin glitches and landing again enters Level 2 (\"The Archive\").\nlet level1Done = jumpToLevel2;\n\nconst planetView = createPlanetView(renderer, {\n  onIslandClick: () => switchTo(level1Done ? archiveView : islandView),\n});\nconst islandView = createIslandView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n  onComplete: () => { level1Done = true; },\n});\nconst archiveView = createArchiveView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n});"
}

{
  "command": "node --input-type=module -e \"\nimport { createTerrain } from './src/terrain.js';\nconst terrain = createTerrain({ size: 200, segments: 220, maxHeight: 26, seed: 1337 });\nfunction mulberry32(seed){return function(){seed|=0;seed=(seed+0x6d2b79f5)|0;let t=Math.imul(seed^(seed>>>15),1|seed);t=(t+Math.imul(t^(t>>>7),61|t))^t;return((t^(t>>>14))>>>0)/4294967296;};}\nconst CONSOLE_POS={x:-6,z:56};\nconst rand=mulberry32(20260609);\nconst placements=[];let spacing=13,tries=0,total=0;\nwhile(placements.length<21){\n  total++;\n  if(++tries>3000){spacing=Math.max(6,spacing-2);tries=0;}\n  const ang=rand()*Math.PI*2, r=16+rand()*56;\n  const x=Math.cos(ang)*r, z=Math.sin(ang)*r;\n  if(terrain.heightAt(x,z)<1.8)continue;\n  if(Math.hypot(x-CONSOLE_POS.x,z-CONSOLE_POS.z)<14)continue;\n  if(placements.some((p)=>Math.hypot(x-p.x,z-p.z)<spacing))continue;\n  placements.push({x,z});\n}\nconst hs=placements.map(p=>terrain.heightAt(p.x,p.z));\nlet minPair=Infinity;\nfor(let i=0;i<21;i++)for(let j=i+1;j<21;j++)minPair=Math.min(minPair,Math.hypot(placements[i].x-placements[j].x,placements[i].z-placements[j].z));\nconsole.log('placed:',placements.length,'attempts:',total,'final spacing:',spacing);\nconsole.log('height min/max:',Math.min(...hs).toFixed(2),Math.max(...hs).toFixed(2));\nconsole.log('closest pair:',minPair.toFixed(1),'min dist to console:',Math.min(...placements.map(p=>Math.hypot(p.x-CONSOLE_POS.x,p.z-CONSOLE_POS.z))).toFixed(1));\n\"",
  "description": "Verify archive block placement terminates and stays on dry land"
}

185	### Out of scope for L1 (later levels)
186	Juggling multiple memories / tighter timers; the "go back / reapply a checkpoint"
187	payoff; recap / activity / dispatch; win/lose beyond the power meter.
188	
189	## Level 2 design — "The Archive" (agreed 2026-06-09, NOT built yet)
190	
191	**Design rule (user, 2026-06-09): one new command per level, and it must
192	visibly show the value of checkpoints.** Level 2's command is
193	**`entire checkpoint search`**. Nothing else is new.
194	
195	Rejected on the way here (kept for the record so we don't re-tread):
196	- An elaborate "corruption" level teaching search + explain + rewind with
197	  clue-hunting, decoy checkpoints, and a spreading corruption zone — **too many
198	  variables to consume at once**.
199	- `rewind` as the L2 command — in the simple framings it kept mis-modeling the
200	  tool (reading as "restore from backup" when real rewind means "take my
201	  *current* state back to a save point; the bank itself is never at risk").
202	- `recap` — L1's three collected items don't warrant a recap; manufacturing the
203	  need (more items, mixed states, a blackout) was creeping back toward
204	  variable-overload.
205	
206	### The fiction
207	Level 1's win restored the ship's memory — side effect: the ship's **entire
208	pre-crash checkpoint archive surfaces**. The island is now dotted with ~20
209	identical dark frozen memories (ice blocks, no beams, unreadable from a
210	distance). Most were never grabbed by the player — the corpus is deliberately
211	**bigger than your head**, because that's the situation search exists for.
212	The player's 3 L1 checkpoints can appear in the archive as a continuity touch.
213	
214	### The loop (×3, one countdown clock for tension)
215	1. **The ship asks** for a memory in a plain sentence that contains the
216	   keyword: *"I need the memory of the **ignition sequence**."* (No clue
217	   hunting — the search word is right there in the request.)
218	2. **Player types** `entire checkpoint search "ignition"` at the terminal.
219	3. **The matching block's beam lights up** across the island — the money shot:
220	   a dark field, one typed word, one light. (In L1 the game lit the beams for
221	   you; in L2 nothing lights until YOU search.)
222	4. **Sprint over, press E** → memory transmits to the ship, power meter
223	   climbs, next request.
224	
225	Wrong/misspelled word → 0 results, no penalty except the ticking clock (which
226	is exactly the real-world cost of bad search terms). Searching by walking the
227	field and inspecting blocks one-by-one stays *possible* but ruinously slow —
228	the tool isn't a gate, it's the obviously rational move.
229	
230	The teaching, felt not told: **your history quickly outgrows your memory;
231	you remember a *word*, not a place — search turns the word into the moment.**
232	Arc so far: L1 — bank it. L2 — search it. (recap / dispatch / resume / rewind
233	reserved for later levels.)
234	
235	### Deliberately NOT in Level 2
236	No clue collection, no decoy results, no timestamps, no `explain`, no rewind
237	browser, no new artifact states. The ~20 archive blocks are set dressing, not
238	state to track. Retrieval is a plain E-grab — the level's typing effort lives
239	in the search command itself (leaning: keep it that simple rather than rerun
240	L1's add/commit beat per item; revisit after a playtest).
241	
242	### Cleanup owed before building
243	An abandoned corruption-level implementation sits uncommitted in the working
244	tree (`src/corruptionView.js`, `#l2-hud` markup in `index.html`, L2 styles in
245	`style.css`, view wiring in `main.js`) — strip it. Keep from that work:
246	`src/memoryProps.js` (shared ice/beam/ID-sprite props), the `onComplete` hook
247	in `islandView.js`, the `#fp-shared` HUD split, and the corrupted-pin entry
248	concept (after L1 completes, the orbit pin glitches and landing again enters
249	Level 2; `?view=level2` / `?level=2` dev shortcuts).
250	
251	## Architecture / where things live
252	
253	```
254	index.html         # canvas + HUD + crossfade overlay + FP prompt + panel + loader
255	src/style.css      # HUD / panel / loader / fade / crosshair / FP-prompt styling
256	src/main.js        # VIEW MANAGER: owns renderer + resize + shared panel + the
257	                   #   render loop; crossfades between the two views.
258	                   #   ?view=island jumps straight onto the island (dev aid).
259	src/planetView.js  # orbit view: scene/lighting/stars/planet/clouds/atmo/ring +
```

Keep from that work:\n`src/memoryProps.js` (shared ice/beam/ID-sprite props), the `onComplete` hook\nin `islandView.js`, the `#fp-shared` HUD split, and the corrupted-pin entry\nconcept (after L1 completes, the orbit pin glitches and landing again enters\nLevel 2; `?view=level2` / `?level=2` dev shortcuts).",

Specifics:\n- **21 archive blocks** (3 targets + 15 old-crew checkpoints + the 3 L1\n  recoveries with the dev-scaffold ids), scattered deterministically on dry\n  land (seeded RNG, min spacing, never in the sea or on the console).\n- Blocks are dark and anonymous; an id sprite appears only when lit, grabbed,\n  or walked up close (~18u) — browsing on foot stays possible but slow.\n- The ship's request panel (top-left, under the clock) holds the keyword in a\n  plain sentence. Terminal (gold beam, auto-opens like L1) accepts\n  `entire checkpoint search \"<word>\"` and `entire checkpoint list` (prints all\n  21 — \"too many to read\" — the why-search-exists beat).\n- Search lights every match's cyan beam (previous search goes dark); E on the\n  requested block transmits it (warm gold); E on a lit-but-wrong block costs\n  only the re-read. 0 results cost only clock.\n- One 90s countdown (tunable `TOTAL_TIME`), same panic-sky as L1, fail = R to\n  retry, win = \"ARCHIVE LINKED\" + B to orbit."
}

> AGENT

Level 2 "The Archive" is built and the corruption code is gone. `npm run build` passes; I did no browser testing — it's ready for you to playtest at `?view=level2` (or `?level=2`, or by finishing Level 1 and clicking the glitching pin).

**What's in the level** (`src/archiveView.js`, ~600 lines):

- **21 dark ice blocks** scattered across the same island — 3 answer the ship's requests, 15 are old-crew filler, and 3 are your Level 1 recoveries (using the dev-scaffold ids for continuity). Placement is seeded-RNG so it's identical every run; I verified in Node that all 21 land on dry ground with proper spacing.
- **The loop ×3**: the ship's request sits top-left under the clock in a plain sentence containing the keyword ("…the old crew tuned the **ignition** timing — send me that moment"). At the gold-beam terminal you type `entire checkpoint search "ignition"`, the matching block's cyan beam lights up across the island, you sprint over and press E to transmit. Blocks are anonymous until lit, grabbed, or walked right up to — so browsing on foot stays possible but hopeless.
- **One 90-second countdown** (`TOTAL_TIME`, tunable) with Level 1's same panic sky. Bad searches return 0 results and cost only clock. Searching a word that matches a *different* block (e.g. "nav" hits the cracked nav panel) lights it honestly; E on it just says it's not what the ship asked for.
- **`entire checkpoint list` still works** — it prints all 21 and tells you "too many to read; search for the one the ship wants." That's the why-search-exists beat, available but never required.
- Win is "ARCHIVE LINKED" with the reveal line *"every moment ever banked is one keyword away"*; timeout is "TRANSFER INCOMPLETE", R to retry.

**Cleanup done as planned**: deleted `src/corruptionView.js`, replaced the `#l2-hud` markup and stripped corruption CSS (corrupt meter, clue cards, glitch flash, rewind-browser rows). Kept `memoryProps.js`, the `onComplete` hook, the `#fp-shared` split, the glitching pin, the dev shortcuts, and the Codex dev scaffold in `islandView.js` untouched. PLAN.md's Level 2 section now records the implementation details.

One thing to watch in your playtest: whether 90 seconds is the right tension for three search-and-sprint round trips — it's a single constant at the top of the file if it needs tuning.

> DEVELOPER

i wrote in the word that I should search for..im confused on like how to actually find the iteem. dont make any changes just explain to me what's going on

> AGENT

Here's the intended flow, end to end — and a guess at where it's tripping you up:

1. **At the terminal** you have to type the whole command, not just the word: `entire checkpoint search "ignition"`. If you only type `ignition` and hit Enter, the terminal rejects it with "command not recognized." (Quotes are optional — `entire checkpoint search ignition` also works.)

2. **When the search hits**, two things happen at once:
   - The terminal prints the matching checkpoint's id + summary as a result row, and flashes "1 checkpoint matched — a beam just lit on the island."
   - Out in the world, the matching ice block lights a **cyan vertical beam** — a tall pillar of light, same look as Level 1's gold memory beams. That beam is the only "map marker" there is; the terminal text doesn't tell you where the block is.

3. **Press Esc** to close the terminal (it holds you in place while it's open), then physically run toward the cyan beam. It might be behind you — turn around and scan the horizon for it.

4. **At the lit block, press E** to transmit it to the ship. Then back to the terminal for request 2.

So the likely confusion is one of these two:

- **You typed just the keyword** — the terminal needs the full `entire checkpoint search "<word>"` command. The CTA under the input line shows that shape.
- **The search worked but you didn't connect the result to the world** — the terminal result is just text; the actual "item" is the ice block out on the island whose beam lit up. Nothing comes to you; you go to it.

Honestly, if step 2 didn't read as "now go find the light," that's a useful playtest signal — the success message may need to be louder about *look up, follow the cyan beam* (or the terminal could even auto-close on a hit). But no changes made — tell me what you actually saw on screen after you hit Enter and I'll know which of the two it was.

> DEVELOPER

so the story here is level 1..you land on a planet..you find all these things that fell out your ship..you grab them..and save them essentially with entire checkpoint..and then whats the story for level 2

> AGENT

Level 2's story picks up right where Level 1's win leaves off:

**Level 1** — you crash-land, the impact scattered the ship's memory across the island, and you save each fragment: commit freezes it in ice, linking a checkpoint banks it into the ship's memory. The ship comes back online.

**Level 2** — restoring the ship's memory had a side effect: it surfaced the ship's *entire pre-crash archive*. This ship had a crew before you, and they checkpointed everything they ever did — engine tunings, star charts, hull repairs, even the galley inventory. So when you land again, the island is dotted with ~20 dark, identical ice blocks: every memory the old crew ever banked, including your own three from Level 1 sitting among them.

The ship is awake now, and it's still broken in specific ways — engines won't fire, nav core is blank, comms can't reach the relay. It *knows* the fixes exist somewhere in that archive, because the old crew solved these problems once before. But it can't tell you which block; all it remembers is a fragment of each moment: "the old crew tuned the *ignition* timing — send me that moment." Your job is to take that scrap of a sentence, search the archive by the word in it, and the right block answers by lighting its beam. Run over, transmit it up, and the ship gets one more piece of its old self back. Three memories, one countdown.

The narrative point mirrors the tool's point: in Level 1 you learned that saving is cheap — bank everything. Level 2 is the consequence of that habit: the bank quickly grows bigger than anyone's head, and standing in a field of identical saved moments, the only thing you actually remember is *a word*. `entire checkpoint search` is how a word becomes the moment again. Level 1 teaches putting memories in; Level 2 teaches getting them back out.

> DEVELOPER

meh ..it just feels like too much..lets take a step back and think about the day to day agentic workflow..problems people have and how entire solves it

> AGENT

Good instinct — let's ground this in what a real day with an agent actually feels like, because that's where the game should be pulling from.

**A day in the agentic workflow, and where it hurts:**

1. **"Go ahead, do it"… with a flinch.** You hand the agent a task and it starts touching files fast. The core daily emotion is *fear of letting it run* — it might confidently rewrite half a module and leave you somewhere worse than where you started. Entire's answer: checkpoints happen automatically behind every commit, so there's always a safe point. `entire checkpoint rewind` takes your *current working state* back to one. That's a save-point reload — and it's the thing that makes people willing to let the agent cook at all.

2. **"Wait, what did it just do?"** The agent did 15 things while you were on a call. Git shows you the diff, but not the reasoning. → `entire recap` / `entire checkpoint explain` — the session behind the commit.

3. **"We solved this before… somewhere."** Weeks later you remember a *word*, not a commit. → `entire checkpoint search`. (This is what L2 currently teaches.)

4. **"New session, who dis."** Context window dies, next morning, new machine — you re-explain everything to the agent from scratch. → `entire session resume`.

5. **Handing it off.** Teammate or future-you needs the story of what happened. → `entire dispatch`.

Here's what jumps out when I line that up against the game: **the game has no agent in it.** The player does everything by hand — but the product's whole reason to exist is *delegation*. Problem #1 is the most visceral, most daily pain, and it's also Entire's emotional core: *you can trust the agent because you can always get back.* The current Archive level teaches problem #3, which is real but a weeks-later pain, not a today pain — which might be exactly why it feels like "too much story" propping up too little feeling.

And note: when we rejected rewind earlier, we rejected a *bad fiction* (storm melts your bank / corruption spreads), not the command. The honest framing was the problem: rewind never threatens the bank — it discards your *current mess* and returns to the save point.

So the simplest L2 that matches real life might be: **the ship gives you a helper drone.** You checkpoint, then tell the drone to build/fix something. It works fast — and sometimes does it visibly, comically wrong. You don't fix its mess by hand; you `entire checkpoint rewind` and let it try again. One new command, the save points from L1 finally *pay off*, and the lesson is felt, not told: *delegation is safe when you've banked the moment before it.* Search slides to a later level, where an archive of accumulated checkpoints will have been earned naturally.

That's my read — the day-to-day arc is **save → trust → recover**, and L2 should probably be the "trust + recover" beat. Want me to sketch what the drone level would look like concretely before we touch any code?

> DEVELOPER

umm dont make any changes..what about the idea of you get a drone..you have it upgrading stuff..somehow u might earn options to add more drones...fixing stuff in tandem..and then there's some reason why you need an explanation of what each drone did..and you will have to run entire checkpoint explain..idk what would be the reason..and how you would get to that end point..but like you need to explanation to complete the parts of the game..any ideas for refinement

> AGENT

No changes — let's just think. The piece you're missing ("why would I *need* the explanation?") is actually the easiest part to steal from real life, because there's exactly one moment in the real workflow where explain is non-optional: **you're about to depend on work you didn't watch happen.** That's code review of an agent's output. So the game needs a moment where the player must *act on* or *vouch for* the drones' work, sight unseen. A few concrete shapes for that:

**1. The trust gate (review before power-on).** The drones finish their upgrades and each one's work is sealed under a checkpoint id. Then the ship balks: *"Three things changed inside me and I didn't witness any of them. I won't ignite until you tell me they're safe."* You run `entire checkpoint explain <id>` on each. Two reports read clean; one is self-evidently wrong — *"hull breach patched · material: galley foil · torque spec: unknown — improvised."* You send that drone back to redo it, explain the new checkpoint, clean, ship powers up, win. The *reading* is the gameplay: the wrong one is obvious **only if you actually read it**, which is exactly the lesson — agent work looks identical from the outside; explain is how you see inside.

**2. The explanation contains a value you need.** The ship asks: *"The drone retuned my antenna — to what frequency? I have to match it or we broadcast into nothing."* The answer exists nowhere in the world — not on the drone, not on the part — only in the checkpoint's explanation. You explain, read "set relay to 117.4," type 117.4 into the ship console. Explain becomes a key, not homework. Repeat ×3 with different drones/values.

**3. The mismatch hunt.** Something's subtly off after the upgrades (a gauge flickers). All three work sites look identical. Explain all three, spot which drone's story doesn't match what it was asked to do, redo that one. This is real-life "which agent change broke the build" — but it's also drifting back toward the corruption level's triage shape, so I'd treat it with caution.

My honest ranking: **#1, with a pinch of #2.** The trust gate is the truest to life (it *is* reviewing the agent's PR before merging), and folding in one #2-style beat ("read the value, act on it") keeps it from being passive reading. #3 we've been burned on before.

On the rest of your sketch — the parts that are strong and the one trap:

- **Earning drones one at a time is great pacing**: drone 1 while you watch (novelty), drone 2 means two things happen at once, drone 3 means you literally cannot witness everything — which *manufactures the need for explain* instead of lore-dumping it. The level's first half creates the problem the command solves in the second half.
- **The tandem moment is the emotional core**: standing on a hill watching three beams working at once while you do nothing. That's the delegation feeling no slide deck gives you.
- **The trap is variable creep**: every drone must be the *same single interaction* (point at broken thing, press E, drone goes). Drones differing in abilities, upgrade trees, drone management — that's the corruption mistake wearing a different hat. More drones should add *parallelism*, never new mechanics.
- **One rule check**: this level's one new command is `explain`. The "redo it" beat should just be the drone re-doing the job in fiction — not `rewind` — or we're teaching two commands again.

And it quietly fixes the arc problem from yesterday: after a drone level, your checkpoint history has *grown by itself* — so a later search level ("where's that thing a drone did last week?") inherits a real archive instead of a lore-dumped one. Bank → delegate & review → search. Each level's history feeds the next.

Want me to write this up as a candidate L2 in PLAN.md next to the Archive design, or keep kicking it around first?

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

Fair. Let me shrink it to L1's size — one new thing, one tiny loop, repeated three times:

**You get one drone. It fixes things. Sometimes wrong. Rewind fixes the drone.**

1. Ship hands you a drone. One interaction: walk to a broken thing, press E, the drone swarms it and "fixes" it.
2. Sometimes the fix comes out visibly wrong — wrong part, sparks, a panel bolted on upside down.
3. One new command at the terminal: `entire checkpoint rewind`. The world snaps back to the moment before the drone touched it. Drone retries, gets it right.
4. Three fixes before the clock. That's the level.

No earning drones, no reports, no values to read, no finding which one — one drone, one command, and the botched fix is unmissable (it's on fire). The lesson is pure save-point: *let the helper run; going back costs one command.*

Explain, search, multiple drones — all later levels, each waiting until the history they need has piled up naturally.

That's about as small as a level can get while still having a beat. Right size now, or still too much?

> DEVELOPER

no i want to be able to get the earning drones. like i can send out multiple..i just thought the parts of entire checkpoint explain were too complicated

> AGENT

Got it — keep the drone fleet, ditch the puzzle. The complicated part was making you *deduce* something from the explanations (find the bad one, extract a value). So drop deduction entirely and make explain the **collection beat itself**, same role the y/n checkpoint played in L1:

1. Fix the first broken thing yourself, L1-style → the ship rewards you with a drone.
2. Point the drone at a broken thing, press E, it flies off and works. While it works, you fix another yourself → earn drone 2, then 3. Soon three repair beams are running at once while you're elsewhere — the tandem moment you wanted.
3. Here's the hook: when a drone finishes, you weren't there. Its work site just seals under a checkpoint id — you have no idea what it actually did.
4. The ship won't power up a system it can't account for. So per drone, one command: `entire checkpoint explain <id>` → a little story card pops up — *"drone-2 · replaced coolant pump · 3 attempts · 2 parts scavenged"* — and that system comes online, meter climbs.
5. All systems explained → win.

Nothing to figure out, nothing to compare — explain is just how you *claim* work that happened without you. Same shape as L1: do, then run the command that makes it count. The teaching lands anyway: every checkpoint has a story behind it, and the story survives even when you weren't watching.

One new command, one repeated beat, drones multiply the chaos without adding mechanics. (The "one drone botched it" twist can always become a later level once this loop is proven.)

That feel right? If yes I can write it into PLAN.md as the new L2 candidate — and we should also decide what happens to the built Archive level (keep as a future search level vs. rip out).

> DEVELOPER

i think this you start with okay you can send out a drone..you tell it to go to something..there's a countdown happening so you can send out as many drones as you want an assign them to different fixes/upgrades..when each one is done..you run over to it..and get an explanation of what was fixed ..once you get the explanation you get a button that it's safe to add to the ship and you press add to ship..u can make the choice to do one drone at a time or send out many drones..i also dont want to type out the checkpoint id when i do entire checkpoint explain

> AGENT

That clicks — and notice what you just designed: the player becomes the *review loop*. Drones do the work in parallel; the thing only YOU can do is walk up, read what happened, and approve it. That's literally the agentic workflow — delegation is cheap, review is the human bottleneck — felt as gameplay, not told. Here's the loop played back:

1. **Briefing**: the ship is broken into ~5–6 systems scattered across the island. You have a drone bay. One countdown for the whole level.
2. **Assign**: walk to a broken site, press E → a drone flies out and starts working (visible progress — sparks, a work beam). You're free immediately.
3. **Your choice**: babysit one drone at a time, or sprint site to site spinning up the whole fleet. The clock makes parallel the obviously rational move — nothing forces it, the math does (same principle as search in the Archive design).
4. **Drone finishes** → the site seals under a checkpoint. You weren't there. You don't know what it did.
5. **Review**: run over → the explanation shows what was actually fixed and how — *"replaced coolant pump · 3 attempts · 2 parts scavenged from the wreck."*
6. **Accept**: an **ADD TO SHIP** button appears (or press Y, matching L1's confirm). Press it → that system comes online, ship meter climbs.
7. All systems added before 0:00 → win.

On not typing the id — easy, and there's a version that stays honest to the real CLI: **walking up to a finished site pre-fills the terminal**. It opens already showing `entire checkpoint explain 31f0cafe4d12` and you just press Enter. You never type an id, but you still *see* that explain takes one — so when you use the real CLI, nothing surprises you. (L1 already set this precedent: you typed `git commit` with no message or files.) If even Enter-on-a-prefilled-line feels like friction we can drop to fully automatic, but I'd start there.

Two small knobs, then it's fully specified:

- **Drone supply**: unlimited (one per site, simplest) vs. a fleet of 3 that free up as they finish (creates a pipeline rhythm: assign, review, reassign). I'd start unlimited — fewer variables — and add scarcity later only if the level feels too easy.
- **The win line**: does the last ADD TO SHIP end the level, or do you cap it L1-style with one `entire checkpoint list` showing everything the fleet did today? The list reprise is a nice "look how much got done without you" moment, but it's an extra beat — your call.

This also resolves explain's "too complicated" problem cleanly: there's nothing to deduce, but the explanation isn't skippable decoration either — it's the gate to the button.

Want me to write this into PLAN.md as the agreed L2 (and note the built Archive level as a future search level we're shelving for now)?

> DEVELOPER

i like entire checkpoint list..unlimited drones..well like the number of drones available should be the number of elements we're going to have..how many elements will we do

> AGENT

I'd say **5**. Here's the reasoning:

- **3** is just L1 again — parallelism never gets interesting, you could almost babysit each drone serially and still win.
- **8+** turns the review phase into a chore loop — five-plus sprints to assign, five-plus to review, the repetition outlasts the fun.
- **5** is where the math becomes *felt*: if each fix takes ~20 seconds of drone time, doing them one-at-a-time costs 100+ seconds of watching — impossible on the clock — while assigning all five up front collapses it to one overlapping wave. The moment you stand on a hill and see five work beams running at once is the level's money shot, and five reads as "a fleet" where three reads as "a few."

It also maps cleanly onto ship fiction — five systems, 20% on the ship meter per ADD TO SHIP:

1. **Ignition coils** (engines)
2. **Nav core**
3. **Long-range antenna** (comms)
4. **Life support scrubbers**
5. **Landing struts** (hull)

(Conveniently echoes the broken-ship complaints from the Archive level, so the universe stays coherent.)

So the locked design: 5 broken systems, 5 drones in the bay (one per job, no economy), one countdown, assign with E, review with a pre-filled `entire checkpoint explain` + Enter, accept with ADD TO SHIP, and the finish line is `entire checkpoint list` at the ship terminal showing all five drone checkpoints — "look how much got done without you."

Writing it into PLAN.md now as the agreed L2, with the Archive level noted as a shelved future search level — then it's ready to build whenever you say go.

189	## Level 2 design — "The Archive" (agreed 2026-06-09, built 2026-06-09)
190	
191	**Design rule (user, 2026-06-09): one new command per level, and it must

  "old_string": "## Level 2 design — \"The Archive\" (agreed 2026-06-09, built 2026-06-09)\n\n**Design rule (user, 2026-06-09): one new command per level, and it must\nvisibly show the value of checkpoints.** Level 2's command is\n**`entire checkpoint search`**. Nothing else is new.",
  "new_string": "## Level 2 design — \"The Drone Bay\" (agreed 2026-06-10, NOT built yet)\n\n**Design rule (user, 2026-06-09): one new command per level, and it must\nvisibly show the value of checkpoints.** Level 2's command is\n**`entire checkpoint explain`**. Nothing else is new.\n\nAfter playtesting the built Archive level (below), Rizel stepped back to first\nprinciples: ground each level in the day-to-day agentic workflow. The daily\narc is **delegate → review → accept**, and the game had no agent in it. So\nLevel 2 puts the player on the review side of delegation — the one part of\nthe loop only the human does.\n\n### The fiction\nThe ship is broken into **5 systems**, each a damaged work site scattered\nacross the island: ignition coils, nav core, long-range antenna, life support\nscrubbers, landing struts. The ship gives you a **drone bay with 5 drones**\n(one per job — no economy, no earning). Drones fix things fast, but the ship\nwon't power up work nobody can account for.\n\n### The loop (one countdown for the whole level)\n1. **Assign**: walk to a broken site, press E → a drone flies out and works\n   on it (visible work beam / sparks). You're free immediately.\n2. **Player's choice**: babysit one drone at a time, or sprint site-to-site\n   launching the whole fleet. Nothing forces parallelism — the clock makes it\n   the obviously rational move (same principle as search: the tool isn't a\n   gate, it's the rational choice). Five beams running at once while you do\n   nothing is the level's money shot.\n3. **Drone finishes** → the site seals under a checkpoint id. You weren't\n   there; you don't know what it did.\n4. **Review**: walk up to a finished site → the terminal opens **pre-filled**\n   with `entire checkpoint explain <id>` — press Enter (no typing ids; you\n   still SEE that real explain takes an id). A story card shows what was\n   actually done: \"replaced coolant pump · 3 attempts · 2 parts scavenged\".\n5. **Accept**: an **ADD TO SHIP** confirm appears (Y, matching L1's confirm\n   beat) → that system comes online, ship meter +20%.\n6. **Finish line**: all five accepted → run `entire checkpoint list` at the\n   ship terminal — all five drone checkpoints, \"look how much got done\n   without you.\" Then the win screen.\n\nFail = clock hits 0:00 before all five are accepted → R to retry.\n\n### Deliberately NOT in Level 2\nNo bad/botched drone work (no deduction, nothing to compare — explain is the\ngate to the ADD TO SHIP button, not a puzzle), no drone management or upgrade\nmechanics (more drones = parallelism, never new mechanics), no typing\ncheckpoint ids, no values to extract from explanations. The \"one drone did it\nwrong — find it\" twist is reserved as a possible later level once this loop\nis proven.\n\n### The teaching, felt not told\nDelegation is cheap and parallel; review is the human bottleneck — and every\ncheckpoint carries the story of work you didn't watch. Arc: L1 — bank it.\nL2 — delegate & review it. Search/rewind/recap/dispatch reserved for later\nlevels, by which point the checkpoint history will have grown naturally.\n\n## Level ? design — \"The Archive\" (built 2026-06-09, SHELVED for a later level)\n\nBuilt as Level 2, then shelved 2026-06-10 after a playtest: the story carried\ntoo much weight (\"meh, it just feels like too much\") and search is a\nweeks-later pain, not a day-to-day one. The implementation is kept intact in\n`src/archiveView.js` (still wired as the post-L1 level until the Drone Bay\nreplaces it) — it should return as a later search level, by which point the\nplayer's checkpoint history (incl. drone work) will have grown enough that\nsearch needs no lore-dump. Level 2's command was\n**`entire checkpoint search`**."
}

> AGENT

PLAN.md is updated. The new Level 2 — **"The Drone Bay"** — is written up as the agreed design (not built yet), and the Archive level is moved to its own shelved section.

The locked spec, as we settled it:

- **5 broken ship systems** across the island (ignition coils, nav core, antenna, life support scrubbers, landing struts), **5 drones** — one per job, no economy.
- **Assign** with E; drones work visibly while you're free to launch the rest or watch one. The single countdown makes parallel the rational choice — five work beams at once is the money shot.
- **Review**: walk to a finished site → terminal opens **pre-filled** with `entire checkpoint explain <id>`, just press Enter — no typing ids, but you still see that the real command takes one. Story card shows what the drone did.
- **Accept**: ADD TO SHIP (Y) → system online, meter +20%.
- **Finish line**: `entire checkpoint list` at the ship terminal showing all five drone checkpoints, then the win screen.
- Explicitly excluded: botched-drone puzzles, drone management, value-extraction from explanations — all the stuff that felt too complicated.

PLAN.md also records *why* the Archive got shelved (story carried too much; search is a weeks-later pain, not a daily one) and that `src/archiveView.js` stays intact to return as a later search level, once the player's history has grown naturally from drone work.

Say go and I'll build it — and when I do, the Drone Bay takes over the post-Level-1 slot from the Archive in `main.js`.

> DEVELOPER

what are they going to upgrade to? like what will the different ship systems upgrade to..for some i was thinking it could be like a bow turns into a sword type of thing

> AGENT

Oh this is the right question — because the transformation is what makes the review beat *work*. If the drone just repairs the thing, "explain" is a receipt. But if you come back and the broken dish is now a glowing lance pointed at the sky, your first thought is *"…what did you DO?"* — and that's exactly the urge `entire checkpoint explain` exists to satisfy. The surprise creates the question; the card answers it. Drones don't repair — they *improvise*.

Here's a before → after for all five, in your bow→sword spirit (and all buildable with the low-poly kit we already have):

1. **Ignition coils → Plasma ring.** Before: a charred coil stack coughing smoke. After: the coils are gone — a floating gold **torus** pulses over the site like a tiny stargate. Card: *"coils beyond saving · rebuilt as a plasma ring from salvaged hull plate · 2 attempts."*

2. **Nav core → Star dome.** Before: a dark, cracked gyroscope sphere. After: a **miniature starfield** projected above it — slowly rotating constellation of points with a ring orbiting through (Points cloud + rings, easy and gorgeous). Card: *"core unrecoverable · remapped from scratch · starfield calibrated to the lavender belt."*

3. **Antenna → Signal spire.** The purest bow→sword: a bent dish lying in the dirt becomes a tall thin **lance firing a beam straight up to orbit** — we already have beam tech, this one's the skyline-changer you see from across the island. Card: *"dish unsalvageable · respun the mast into a spire · relay handshake at full strength."*

4. **Life support scrubbers → Garden pod.** Before: a rusted vent box leaking gray haze. After: a **glass dome with glowing plants inside**, soft green motes drifting out — the only living green on the lavender island. Card: *"filters dead · replaced with a living filter · two vines scavenged from the crash."*

5. **Landing struts → Grav skids.** Before: a collapsed, twisted leg. After: the strut is gone and a **hover pad floats a half-meter off the ground**, bobbing gently over a cyan glow ring. Card: *"strut seized solid · swapped for grav skids · technically the ship floats now."*

The visual grammar ties it together: broken sites smolder gray → an assigned drone works in cyan sparks → a *finished* site seals the new object in the familiar ice + checkpoint id (continuity: drones bank their work exactly like you did in L1) → explain + ADD TO SHIP melts the ice and the upgrade switches on in gold. Five weird new monuments on the island by the end, and the final `entire checkpoint list` reads like a tour of them.

It also feeds the meta-story: the ship you eventually fly home isn't the ship that crashed — it's stranger and better, made of five improvisations you approved but didn't perform.

Want me to lock these five into PLAN.md as the upgrade table?

> DEVELOPER

yes and execute

{

  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\nimport { createTerrain } from \"./terrain.js\";\nimport { createFirstPerson } from \"./firstPerson.js\";\nimport { makeBeamTexture, makeIceBlock, makeIdSprite } from \"./memoryProps.js\";\n\n// LEVEL 2 (\"The Drone Bay\") — the same island, revisited.\n//\n// The day-to-day agentic loop, played: delegate → review → accept.\n// Five ship systems are dark; five drones can fix them in parallel while you\n// can only be in one place. One new command on top of what L1 taught:\n//\n//   walk to a dark system, press E      → a drone takes the job, you're free\n//   the drone finishes while you're away → its work seals under a checkpoint\n//   walk up → `entire checkpoint explain <id>` is PRE-FILLED — press Enter\n//   read what it actually did            → ADD TO SHIP (Y) → system online\n//   all five online → `entire checkpoint list` at the terminal = finish line\n//\n// Drones don't repair — they IMPROVISE (a bent dish comes back as a signal\n// spire). The transformation is what makes you NEED the explanation.\n//\n// PRESSURE: one countdown. Watching one drone at a time can't beat the clock;\n// running the whole fleet in parallel can. Nothing forces it — the math does.\n\nconst INTERACT_DIST = 8;     // how close (XZ) to assign / review a site\nconst CONSOLE_DIST = 9;      // how close (XZ) to open the ship terminal\nconst TOTAL_TIME = 150;      // seconds for the whole run (tunable)\nconst WORK_TIME = 18;        // seconds a drone spends fixing a system\nconst LOW_TIME = 45;         // clock turns urgent under this\nconst CRIT_TIME = 15;        // clock goes CRITICAL under this\nconst PANIC_TIME = 45;       // the SKY starts shifting toward panic-red\nconst DRONE_SPEED = 26;      // world units / second in flight\nconst MELT_DUR = 1.2;        // seconds the ice takes to melt on ADD TO SHIP\n\n// Sky panic palette — same dread as Level 1's clock.\nconst SKY_CALM  = new THREE.Color(0x2a2350);\nconst SKY_PANIC = new THREE.Color(0x6e0f16);\nconst FOG_PANIC = new THREE.Color(0x4a0a0e);\nconst DOME_CALM = new THREE.Color(0x3a3168);\nconst DOME_PANIC = new THREE.Color(0x7a141c);\nconst SUN_CALM  = new THREE.Color(0xfff1dc);\nconst SUN_PANIC = new THREE.Color(0xff5a3c);\n\nconst CONSOLE_POS = { x: -6, z: 56 };\nconst BAY_ROW = { x0: -16, dx: 4, z: 50 };   // five parked drones by the console\n\n// The five dark systems, what the drones improvise them into, and the story\n// each checkpoint carries. Ids are fixed so the fiction is stable across runs.\nconst SYSTEMS = [\n  {\n    ckpt: \"a1c9e4f72b05\", name: \"IGNITION COILS\",\n    pos: { x: -34, z: -28 },\n    broken: \"buildBrokenCoils\", upgrade: \"buildPlasmaRing\",\n    became: \"Plasma Ring\",\n    card: [\n      [\"drone\", \"drone-1\"],\n      [\"system\", \"ignition coils\"],\n      [\"did\", \"coils beyond saving — rebuilt as a plasma ring from salvaged hull plate\"],\n      [\"session\", \"2 attempts · parts scavenged: hull plate ×3\"],\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    ckpt: \"3d7b0f9c61ae\", name: \"NAV CORE\",\n    pos: { x: 40, z: -18 },\n    broken: \"buildBrokenNav\", upgrade: \"buildStarDome\",\n    became: \"Star Dome\",\n    card: [\n      [\"drone\", \"drone-2\"],\n      [\"system\", \"nav core\"],\n      [\"did\", \"core unrecoverable — remapped from scratch; starfield calibrated to the lavender belt\"],\n      [\"session\", \"1 attempt · 412 stars plotted\"],\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    ckpt: \"f25c8b30d971\", name: \"LONG-RANGE ANTENNA\",\n    pos: { x: 28, z: 38 },\n    broken: \"buildBrokenDish\", upgrade: \"buildSignalSpire\",\n    became: \"Signal Spire\",\n    card: [\n      [\"drone\", \"drone-3\"],\n      [\"system\", \"long-range antenna\"],\n      [\"did\", \"dish unsalvageable — respun the mast into a signal spire; relay handshake at full strength\"],\n      [\"session\", \"3 attempts · the first two fell over\"],\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    ckpt: \"62e0a9d4c8f3\", name: \"LIFE SUPPORT\",\n    pos: { x: -30, z: 36 },\n    broken: \"buildBrokenVent\", upgrade: \"buildGardenPod\",\n    became: \"Garden Pod\",\n    card: [\n      [\"drone\", \"drone-4\"],\n      [\"system\", \"life support scrubbers\"],\n      [\"did\", \"filters dead — replaced with a living filter; two vines scavenged from the crash\"],\n      [\"session\", \"1 attempt · the vines approved\"],\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    ckpt: \"8b47f1e62da0\", name: \"LANDING STRUTS\",\n    pos: { x: 8, z: -44 },\n    broken: \"buildBrokenStrut\", upgrade: \"buildGravSkid\",\n    became: \"Grav Skid\",\n    card: [\n      [\"drone\", \"drone-5\"],\n      [\"system\", \"landing struts\"],\n      [\"did\", \"strut seized solid — swapped for grav skids; technically the ship floats now\"],\n      [\"session\", \"2 attempts · torque spec: vibes\"],\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\nfunction normalizeCmd(s) {\n  return s.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\\s+/g, \" \");\n}\n\n// ---------- prop builders: the broken systems ----------\nconst CHARRED = () => new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x2a2e38, roughness: 0.9, metalness: 0.3 });\nconst SCORCH = () => new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x3a3f4a, roughness: 0.85, metalness: 0.25 });\n\nfunction withWarnLight(g) {\n  // A small red blinking bulb so broken things read as \"broken\" up close.\n  const bulb = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.22, 10, 10),\n    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x401512, emissive: 0xff3b2e, emissiveIntensity: 1.6 })\n  );\n  bulb.position.y = g.userData.warnY ?? 3.2;\n  g.add(bulb);\n  g.userData.warn = bulb;\n  return g;\n}\nfunction buildBrokenCoils() {\n  const g = new THREE.Group();\n  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {\n    const c = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.CylinderGeometry(1.1 - i * 0.18, 1.2 - i * 0.18, 1.0, 10), i ? CHARRED() : SCORCH());\n    c.position.y = 0.5 + i * 1.05;\n    c.rotation.z = (i % 2 ? -1 : 1) * 0.12 * (i + 1);   // the stack leans, sadly\n    g.add(c);\n  }\n  g.userData.warnY = 3.6;\n  return withWarnLight(g);\n}\nfunction buildBrokenNav() {\n  const g = new THREE.Group();\n  const ped = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.9, 1.2, 1.4, 8), SCORCH());\n  ped.position.y = 0.7;\n  const core = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.IcosahedronGeometry(1.5, 0), CHARRED());\n  core.position.y = 2.6;\n  core.rotation.set(0.4, 0.2, 0.5);\n  g.add(ped, core);\n  g.userData.warnY = 4.4;\n  return withWarnLight(g);\n}\nfunction buildBrokenDish() {\n  const g = new THREE.Group();\n  const mast = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.18, 0.24, 3.2, 8), SCORCH());\n  mast.position.y = 1.4;\n  mast.rotation.z = 0.5;                                // bent over\n  const dish = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.CylinderGeometry(1.9, 1.5, 0.4, 12), CHARRED());\n  dish.position.set(1.6, 0.5, 0);\n  dish.rotation.z = 1.2;                                // face-down in the dirt\n  g.add(mast, dish);\n  g.userData.warnY = 3.0;\n  return withWarnLight(g);\n}\nfunction buildBrokenVent() {\n  const g leg.rotation.z = 0.55;                                // collapsed sideways\n  const foot = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.CylinderGeometry(1.4, 1.6, 0.5, 10), CHARRED());\n  foot.position.set(1.6, 0.25, THREE.TorusGeometry(1.8, 0.3, 12, 36),\n    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x6b4a16, emissive: 0xffb86b, emissiveIntensity: 1.4, roughness: 0.4 })\n  );\n  ring.position.y = 3.4;\n  g.add(base, ring);\n  g.userData.anim = { type: \"ring\", ring };\n  return g;\n}\nfunction buildStarDome() {\n  const g = new THREE.Group();\n  const ped = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.9, 1.2, 1.2, 8), SCORCH());\n  ped.position.y = 0.6;\n  const stars = new THREE.BufferGeometry();\n  const pts = new Float32Array(120 * 3);\n  for (let i = 0; i < 120; i++) {\n    // random points in a dome above the pedestal\n    const r = 1.2 + Math.random() * 1.4;\n    const th = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;\n    const ph = Math.random() * Math.PI * 0.5;\n    pts[i * 3] = Math.cos(th) * Math.sin(ph) * r;\n    pts[i * 3 + 1] = 3.2 + Math.cos(ph) * r * 0.9;\n    pts[i * 3 + 2] = Math.sin(th) * Math.sin(ph) * r;\n  }\n  stars.setAttribute(\"position\", new THREE.BufferAttribute(pts, 3));\n  const field = new THREE.Points(stars, new THREE.PointsMaterial({\n    color: 0xbfe9ff, size: 0.16, transparent: true, opacity: 0.95,\n    blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending, depthWrite: false,\n  }));\n  const orbit = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.TorusGeometry(2.0, 0.05, 8, 40),\n    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: 0x6fe3ff, transparent: 10, 1, true),\n    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({\n      map: beamTex, color: 0x9af0ff, transparent: true, opacity: 0.55,\n      blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending, depthWrite: false, side: THREE.DoubleSide, fog: false,\n    })\n  );\n  beam.position.y = 42;\n  beam.visible = false;          // fires when the upgrade is ADDED\n  g.add(spire, beam);\n  g.userData.anim = { type: \"spire\", 0.3;\n  for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++) {\n    const plant = new THREE.Mesh(\n      new THREE.ConeGeometry(0.28, 0.9 + (i % 3) * 0.35, 6),\n      new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x1d4a2c, emissive: 0x35d97a, emissiveIntensity: 0.7, roughness: 0.7 })\n    );\n    const a = (i / 5) * Math.PI * 2;\n    plant.position.set(Math.cos(a) * 1.0, 1.0, Math.sin(a) * 1.0);\n    g.add(plant);\n  }\n  const domeGlass = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.SphereGeometry(2.1, 18, 12, 0, Math.PI * 2, 0, Math.PI / 2),\n    new THREE.MeshPhysicalMaterial({\n      color: 0xd8ffe9, metalness: 0, roughness: 0.05, transmission: 0.7,\n      thickness: 1.2, transparent: true, opacity: 0.4,\n    })\n  );\n  domeGlass.position.y = 0.6;\n  g.add(tray, domeGlass);\n  g.userData.anim = { type: \"pod\" };\n  return g;\n}\nfunction buildGravSkid() {\n  const g = new THREE.Group();\n  const pad = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.CylinderGeometry(1.7, 1.9, 0.5, 12),\n    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({
