8, 32),\n    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: 0x6fe3ff, transparent: true, opacity: 0.65 })\n  );\n  glow.rotation.x = Math.PI / 2;\n  glow.position.y = 0.5;\n  g.add(pad, glow);\n  g.userData.anim = { type: \"skid\", pad, glow };\n  return g;\n}\n\n// ---------- the little worker drone ----------\nfunction buildDrone() {\n  const g = new THREE.Group();\n  const body = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.OctahedronGeometry(0.7, 0),\n    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x2c3140, metalness: 0.6, roughness: 0.3, emissive: 0x2a8aa6, emissiveIntensity: 1.0 })\n  );\n  const ring = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.TorusGeometry(1.0, 0.09, 8, 24),\n    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: 0x6fe3ff, transparent: true, opacity: 0.8 })\n  );\n  ring.rotation.x = Math.PI / 2;\n  g.add(body, ring);\n  g.userData = { body, ring };\n  return g;\n}\n\n// The ship console the terminal lives in.\nfunction makeConsole() {\n  const g = new THREE.Group();\n  const pedestal = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.BoxGeometry(2.6, 4.6, 1.6),\n    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x1c2230, metalness: 0.6, roughness: 0.35 })\n  );\n  pedestal.position.y = 2.3;\n  const screen = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.PlaneGeometry(2.1, 1.3),\n    new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({\n      color: 0x0c2030, emissive: 0x2a8aa6, emissiveIntensity: 1.4, roughness: 0.3,\n    })\n  );\n  screen.position.set(0, 4.0, 0.82);\n  screen.rotation.x = -0.28;\n  g.add(pedestal, screen);\n  return g;\n}\n\nexport function createDroneBayView(renderer, { onExit } = {}) {\n  const canvas = renderer.domElement;\n\n  // ---------- scene & sky ----------\n  const scene = new THREE.Scene();\n  scene.background = SKY_CALM.clone();\n  scene.fog = new THREE.Fog(SKY_CALM.clone(), 90, 320);\n\n  const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(\n    62, window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight, 0.1, 2000\n  );\n\n  // ---------- lighting ----------\n  scene.add(new THREE.HemisphereLight(0xcdbcff, 0x3a2f5e, 0.7));\n  const sun = new THREE.DirectionalLight(SUN_CALM, 1.5);\n  sun.position.set(60, 90, 40);\n  scene.add(sun);\n  scene.add(new THREE.AmbientLight(0x6a5a92, 0.3));\n\n  const dome = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.SphereGeometry(900, 32, 16),\n    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: DOME_CALM.clone(), side: THREE.BackSide, fog: false })\n  );\n  scene.add(dome);\n\n  // ---------- terrain (the same island) ----------\n  const terrain = createTerrain({ size: 200, segments: 220, maxHeight: 26, seed: 1337 });\n  scene.add(terrain.mesh, terrain.water);\n\n  const beamTex = makeBeamTexture();\n\n  // ---------- the five work sites ----------\n  const sites = SYSTEMS.map((sys, idx) => {\n    const anchor = new THREE.Group();\n    anchor.position.set(sys.pos.x, terrain.heightAt(sys.pos.x, sys.pos.z), sys.pos.z);\n\n    const BUILD = {\n      buildBrokenCoils, buildBrokenNav, buildBrokenDish, buildBrokenVent, buildBrokenStrut,\n      buildPlasmaRing, buildStarDome, buildGardenPod, buildGravSkid,\n      buildSignalSpire: () => buildSignalSpire(beamTex),\n    };\n    const brokenModel = BUILD[sys.broken]();\n    const upgradeModel = BUILD[sys.upgrade]();\n    upgradeModel.visible = false;\n    anchor.add(brokenModel, upgradeModel);\n\n    // Status beam — readable from across the island:\n    // amber = broken, cyan pulse = drone at work, ice-blue = sealed (review me).\n    const beam = new THREE.Mesh(\n      new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.6, 0.6, 60, 12, 1, true),\n      new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({\n        map: beamTex, color: 0xff8a5c, transparent: true, opacity: 0.3,\n        blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending, depthWrite: false, side: THREE.DoubleSide,\n        fog: false,\n      })\n    );\n    beam.position.y = 30;\n    anchor.add(beam);\n\n    // The checkpoint seal: ice + floating trailer id, like Level 1's bank.\n    const ice = makeIceBlock();\n    ice.scale.setScalar(1.25);\n    ice.position.y = 2.6;\n    ice.visible = false;\n    anchor.add(ice);\n    const sprite = makeIdSprite(sys.ckpt);\n    sprite.position.y = 8.4;\n    sprite.visible = false;\n    anchor.add(sprite);\n\n    scene.add(anchor);\n    return {\n      sys, idx, anchor, brokenModel, upgradeModel, beam, ice, sprite,\n      state: \"broken\",      // broken → working → sealed → added\n      workT: 0,\n      meltT: 0,             // ice melt animation after ADD TO SHIP\n    };\n  });\n\n  // ---------- the drone bay: five parked drones by the console ----------\n  const drones = SYSTEMS.map((_, i) => {\n    const d = buildDrone();\n    const x = BAY_ROW.x0 + i * BAY_ROW.dx;\n    const home = new THREE.Vector3(x, terrain.heightAt(x, BAY_ROW.z) + 1.2, BAY_ROW.z);\n    d.position.copy(home);\n    scene.add(d);\n    return {\n      model: d, home,\n      state: \"bay\",         // bay → flying → working → returning\n      site: null,\n      from: new THREE.Vector3(), to: new THREE.Vector3(),\n      t: 0, dur: 1,\n      bobOff: Math.random() * 10,\n    };\n  });\n\n  // Work beam shown under a working drone (one per drone, toggled).\n  const workBeams = drones.map(() => {\n    const m = new THREE.Mesh(\n      new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.25, 1.2, 6, 10, 1, true),\n      new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({\n        color: 0x6fe3ff, transparent: true, opacity: 0.5,\n        blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending, depthWrite: false, side: THREE.DoubleSide,\n      })\n    );\n    m.visible = false;\n    scene.add(m);\n    return m;\n  });\n\n  // ---------- the ship console ----------\n  const consoleAnchor = new THREE.Group();\n  consoleAnchor.position.set(\n    CONSOLE_POS.x, terrain.heightAt(CONSOLE_POS.x, CONSOLE_POS.z), CONSOLE_POS.z\n  );\n  consoleAnchor.add(makeConsole());\n  const consoleGlow = new THREE.PointLight(0xffd27a, 5, 28, 2);\n  consoleGlow.position.y = 5;\n  consoleAnchor.add(consoleGlow);\n  scene.add(consoleAnchor);\n\n  // ---------- first-person controller ----------\n  const fp = createFirstPerson(camera, canvas, {\n    heightAt: terrain.heightAt,\n    radius: terrain.radius * 0.96,\n    eyeHeight: 2.6,\n    speed: 24,\n  });\n  scene.add(fp.controls.object);\n\n  // ---------- HUD elements (shared FP set + this level's own) ----------\n  const promptEl = document.getElementById(\"fp-prompt\");\n  const controlsEl = document.getElementById(\"fp-controls\");\n  const crosshair = document.getElementById(\"crosshair\");\n  const tutorialEl = document.getElementById(\"tutorial\");\n  const fpShared = document.getElementById(\"fp-shared\");\n  const termEl = document.getElementById(\"terminal\");\n  const termHint = document.getElementById(\"term-hint\");\n  const termInput = document.getElementById(\"term-input\");\n  const termList = document.getElementById(\"term-list\");\n  const termMsg = document.getElementById(\"term-msg\");\n  const termCta = document.getElementById(\"term-cta\");\n  const dbHud = document.getElementById(\"db-hud\");\n  const briefingEl = document.getElementById(\"db-briefing\");\n  const briefingStartBtn = document.getElementById(\"db-briefing-start\");\n  const countdownEl = document.getElementById(\"db-countdown\");\n  const countdownTime = document.getElementById(\"db-countdown-time\");\n  const systemsEl = document.getElementById(\"db-systems-rows\");\n  const winEl = document.getElementById(\"db-win\");\n  const winSub = document.getElementById(\"db-win-sub\");\n  const failEl = document.getElementById(\"db-fail\");\n\n  let active = false;\n  let started = false;\n  let failed = false;\n  let listShown = false;        // `entire checkpoint list` has been run — the win\n  let promptText = null;\n  let controlsLocked = null;\n  const taught = new Set();\n  let tutorialTimer = null;\n  let msgTimer = null;\n  let winTimer = null;\n\n  // Level countdown — the single source of pressure for the whole run.\n  let timeLeft = TOTAL_TIME;\n  let timerRunning = false;\n\n  // Terminal state. Two modes share the one terminal:\n  //  \"review\"  — pre-filled `entire checkpoint explain <id>` at a sealed site\n  //  \"console\" — type `entire checkpoint list` at the ship console (finish line)\n  let terminalOpen = false;\n  let termMode = null;\n  let reviewSite = null;        // the sealed site being reviewed\n  let explained = false;        // Enter pressed — the card is showing\n  let buffer = \"\";\n  let dismissedSite = null;     // site the player Esc'd out of (until they leave)\n  let consoleDismissed = false;\n\n  const addedCount = () => sites.filter((s) => s.state === \"added\").length;\n  const allAdded = () => addedCount() >= sites.length;\n\n  // ---------- HUD helpers ----------\n  function setPrompt(text) {\n    if (!promptEl || text === promptText) return;\n    promptText = text;\n    if (text) { promptEl.textContent = text; promptEl.classList.remove(\"hidden\"); }\n    else promptEl.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n  }\n  function showTutorial(text, ms = 5500) {\n    if (!tutorialEl) return;\n    clearTimeout(tutorialTimer);\n    tutorialEl.textContent = text;\n    tutorialEl.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    if (ms > 0) tutorialTimer = setTimeout(() => tutorialEl.classList.add(\"hidden\"), ms);\n  }\n  function teachOnce(key, text, ms) {\n    if (taught.has(key)) return;\n    taught.add(key);\n    showTutorial(text, ms);\n  }\n  function setControls(locked) {\n    if (!controlsEl) return;\n    if (locked === controlsLocked && !controlsEl.classList.contains(\"hidden\")) return;\n    controlsLocked = locked;\n    controlsEl.innerHTML = `\n      <span class=\"control-item\">\n        <span class=\"control-label\">To move</span>\n        <span class=\"arrow-keys\" aria-label=\"Arrow keys\">\n          <span class=\"key key-up\">↑</span>\n          <span class=\"key key-left\">←</span>\n          <span class=\"key key-down\">↓</span>\n          <span class=\"key key-right\">→</span>\n        </span>\n      </span>\n      ${locked ? `\n        <span class=\"control-item\">\n          <span class=\"control-label\">To look around</span>\n          <span class=\"mouse-hint\">\n            <span class=\"mouse-icon\" aria-hidden=\"true\"></span>\n            <span>move mouse</span>\n          </span>\n        </span>\n      ` : \"\"}\n      <span class=\"control-item\">\n        <span class=\"control-label\">Send a drone</span>\n        <span class=\"key\">E</span>\n      </span>\n      <span class=\"control-item\">\n        <span class=\"control-label\">Return to orbit</span>\n        <span class=\"key\">B</span>\n      </span>\n    `;\n    controlsEl.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n  }\n  function hideControls() {\n    controlsLocked = null;\n    controlsEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n  }\n\n  // ---------- systems status panel ----------\n  const STATE_LABEL = {\n    broken: \"BROKEN\", working: \"DRONE AT WORK\", sealed: \"READY TO REVIEW\", added: \"ONLINE\",\n  };\n  function updateSystems() {\n    if (!systemsEl) return;\n    systemsEl.innerHTML = sites.map((s) =>\n      `<div class=\"db-sys is-${s.state}\">` +\n      `<span class=\"db-sys-name\">${s.sys.name}</span>` +\n      `<span class=\"db-sys-state\">${STATE_LABEL[s.state]}</span></div>`\n    ).join(\"\");\n  }\n\n  // ---------- level countdown ----------\n  function fmtTime(s) {\n    s = Math.max(0, Math.ceil(s));\n    return `${Math.floor(s / 60)}:${String(s % 60).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n  }\n  function updateClock() {\n    if (countdownTime) countdownTime.textContent = fmtTime(timeLeft);\n    countdownEl?.classList.toggle(\"is-low\", timerRunning && timeLeft <= LOW_TIME);\n    countdownEl?.classList.toggle(\"is-critical\", timerRunning && timeLeft <= CRIT_TIME);\n  }\n  function panicFactor() {\n    if (failed) return 1;\n    if (!timerRunning || timeLeft > PANIC_TIME) return 0;\n    let p = (PANIC_TIME - timeLeft) / PANIC_TIME;\n    p *= p;\n    if (timeLeft <= CRIT_TIME) {\n      const throb = 0.5 + 0.5 * Math.sin(performance.now() / 85);\n      p += 0.14 * throb;\n    }\n    return Math.min(1, p);\n  }\n  function applyPanicSky() {\n    const p = listShown ? 0 : panicFactor();\n    scene.background.copy(SKY_CALM).lerp(SKY_PANIC, p);\n    scene.fog.color.copy(SKY_CALM).lerp(FOG_PANIC, p);\n    dome.material.color.copy(DOME_CALM).lerp(DOME_PANIC, p);\n    sun.color.copy(SUN_CALM).lerp(SUN_PANIC, p * 0.85);\n  }\n\n  // ---------- briefing / win / fail / reset ----------\n  function showBriefing() {\n    timerRunning = false;\n    fp.detach();\n    tutorialEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    briefingEl?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n  }\n  function startLevel() {\n    if (started) return;\n    started = true;\n    briefingEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    fp.attach();\n    timeLeft = TOTAL_TIME;\n    timerRunning = true;\n    updateClock();\n    showTutorial(\"Five systems are dark — the amber beams. Walk to one and press E: a drone takes the job while you move on.\", 8500);\n  }\n  briefingStartBtn?.addEventListener(\"click\", startLevel);\n\n  function failLevel() {\n    if (failed || listShown) return;\n    failed = true;\n    timerRunning = false;\n    closeTerminal(false);\n    fp.detach();\n    countdownEl?.classList.remove(\"is-low\", \"is-critical\");\n    tutorialEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    failEl?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n  }\n  function resetLevel() {\n    failed = false;\n    listShown = false;\n    dismissedSite = null;\n    consoleDismissed = false;\n    clearTimeout(winTimer);\n    for (const s of sites) {\n      s.state = \"broken\";\n      s.workT = 0; s.meltT = 0;\n      s.brokenModel.visible = true;\n      s.upgradeModel.visible = false;\n      const anim = s.upgradeModel.userData.anim;\n      if (anim?.type === \"spire\") anim.beam.visible = false;\n      s.ice.visible = false;\n      s.ice.material.opacity = 0.62;\n      s.sprite.visible = false;\n    }\n    for (let i = 0; i < drones.length; i++) {\n      drones[i].state = \"bay\";\n      drones[i].site = null;\n      drones[i].model.position.copy(drones[i].home);\n      workBeams[i].visible = false;\n    }\n    failEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    winEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    updateSystems();\n    timeLeft = TOTAL_TIME;\n    timerRunning = true;\n    updateClock();\n    if (active) fp.attach();\n    showTutorial(\"New attempt — the fleet works in parallel. Use all five drones.\", 6000);\n  }\n\n  // ---------- drone dispatching ----------\n  function assignDrone(site) {\n    const drone = drones.find((d) => d.state === \"bay\");\n    if (!drone) return;                 // shouldn't happen: one drone per system\n    site.state = \"working\";\n    site.workT = 0;\n    drone.state = \"flying\";\n    drone.site = site;\n    drone.from.copy(drone.model.position);\n    drone.to.set(site.anchor.position.x, site.anchor.position.y + 6.5, site.anchor.position.z);\n    drone.t = 0;\n    drone.dur = Math.max(1, drone.from.distanceTo(drone.to) / DRONE_SPEED);\n    updateSystems();\n    teachOnce(\"assigned\",\n      \"The drone's got it — you're free. Send the others while it works; the clock won't wait.\", 7000);\n  }\n  function sealSite(site) {\n    site.state = \"sealed\";\n    site.brokenModel.visible = false;\n    site.upgradeModel.visible = true;   // built — but dormant until ADD TO SHIP\n    site.ice.visible = true;\n    site.ice.material.opacity = 0.62;\n    site.sprite.visible = true;\n    updateSystems();\n    teachOnce(\"sealed\",\n      \"A drone finished while you were away — its work is sealed under a checkpoint. Walk up and see what it actually did.\", 8000);\n  }\n\n  // ---------- terminal ----------\n  function flashTerminal(text, ok) {\n    if (!termMsg) return;\n    clearTimeout(msgTimer);\n    termMsg.textContent = text;\n    termMsg.classList.remove(\"show-ok\", \"show-err\");\n    termMsg.classList.add(ok ? \"show-ok\" : \"show-err\");\n    msgTimer = setTimeout(() => termMsg.classList.remove(\"show-ok\", \"show-err\"), 3600);\n  }\n  function renderTerminal() {\n    if (!terminalOpen) return;\n    if (termMode === \"review\") {\n      if (!explained) {\n        // The command comes pre-filled — you never type a checkpoint id.\n        termHint.textContent = \"# what did the drone actually do? ask the checkpoint\";\n        termInput.textContent = `entire checkpoint explain ${reviewSite.sys.ckpt}`;\n        termInput.classList.remove(\"is-dim\");\n        if (termCta) termCta.innerHTML =\n          `<span class=\"cta-label\">PRESS</span>` +\n          `<kbd class=\"cta-key cta-key-yes\">Enter</kbd><span class=\"cta-note\">run it</span>`;\n      } else {\n        termHint.textContent = \"# reviewed — your call\";\n        termInput.textContent = `entire checkpoint explain ${reviewSite.sys.ckpt}`;\n        termInput.classList.add(\"is-dim\");\n        if (termCta) termCta.innerHTML =\n          `<button id=\"db-add-btn\" type=\"button\">ADD TO SHIP</button>` +\n          `<span class=\"cta-note\">or press <kbd class=\"cta-key cta-key-yes\">Y</kbd></span>`;\n      }\n    } else if (termMode === \"console\") {\n      termHint.textContent = listShown\n        ? \"# the fleet's day, on the record\"\n        : \"# every system accepted — close out the day\";\n      termInput.textContent = buffer;\n      termInput.classList.remove(\"is-dim\");\n      if (termCta) termCta.innerHTML = listShown ? \"\" :\n        `<span class=\"cta-label\">TYPE</span><span class=\"cta-cmd\">entire checkpoint list</span>`;\n    }\n  }\n  function openTerminal(mode, site) {\n    if (terminalOpen) return;\n    terminalOpen = true;\n    termMode = mode;\n    reviewSite = site || null;\n    explained = false;\n    buffer = \"\";\n    fp.detach();\n    termMsg?.classList.remove(\"show-ok\", \"show-err\");\n    termList?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    termEl?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    renderTerminal();\n  }\n  function closeTerminal(dismiss) {\n    if (!terminalOpen) return;\n    if (dismiss) {\n      if (termMode === \"review\" && reviewSite) dismissedSite = reviewSite;\n      if (termMode === \"console\") consoleDismissed = true;\n    }\n    terminalOpen = false;\n    termMode = null;\n    reviewSite = null;\n    explained = false;\n    buffer = \"\";\n    termEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    termList?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    if (active && !failed) fp.attach();\n  }\n\n  // ---------- entire checkpoint explain (pre-filled, Enter to run) ----------\n  function runExplain() {\n    if (!reviewSite || explained) return;\n    explained = true;\n    const s = reviewSite;\n    if (termList) {\n      termList.innerHTML =\n        `<div class=\"term-list-row\"><span class=\"tl-key tl-exp-key\">checkpoint</span>` +\n        `<span class=\"tl-title\">${s.sys.ckpt}</span></div>` +\n        s.sys.card.map(([k, v]) =>\n          `<div class=\"term-list-row\"><span class=\"tl-key tl-exp-key\">${k}</span>` +\n          `<span class=\"tl-title\">${v}</span></div>`\n        ).join(\"\");\n      termList.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    }\n    flashTerminal(`explained ${s.sys.ckpt} — ${s.sys.name.toLowerCase()} is now a ${s.sys.became}`, true);\n    teachOnce(\"explained\",\n      \"That's the story behind the checkpoint — work you didn't watch is never a mystery. If it's good, add it to the ship.\", 8000);\n    renderTerminal();\n  }\n\n  // ---------- ADD TO SHIP ----------\n  function addToShip() {\n    if (!reviewSite || !explained) return;\n    const s = reviewSite;\n    s.state = \"added\";\n    s.meltT = MELT_DUR;                 // the seal melts; the upgrade wakes up\n    const anim = s.upgradeModel.userData.anim;\n    if (anim?.type === \"spire\") anim.beam.visible = true;\n    closeTerminal(false);\n    updateSystems();\n    if (allAdded()) {\n      showTutorial(\"All five online! Run `entire checkpoint list` at the ship terminal — the gold beam — before 0:00.\", 0);\n    } else {\n      showTutorial(`${s.sys.name} online (${addedCount()} / ${sites.length}).`, 5000);\n    }\n  }\n  // The ADD TO SHIP button (terminal CTA is re-rendered, so delegate the click).\n  termCta?.addEventListener(\"click\", (e) => {\n    if (!active) return;\n    if (e.target?.id === \"db-add-btn\") addToShip();\n  });\n\n  // ---------- the finish line: entire checkpoint list ----------\n  function showList() {\n    listShown = true;\n    timerRunning = false;\n    countdownEl?.classList.remove(\"is-low\", \"is-critical\");\n    if (termList) {\n      termList.innerHTML = sites.map((s) =>\n        `<div class=\"term-list-row\"><span class=\"tl-id tl-id-short\">${s.sys.ckpt}</span>` +\n        `<span class=\"tl-title\">drone fix: ${s.sys.name.toLowerCase()} → ${s.sys.became}</span></div>`\n      ).join(\"\");\n      termList.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    }\n    flashTerminal(\"5 checkpoints · 5 upgrades · 1 crew member — look how much got done without you\", true);\n    renderTerminal();\n    if (winSub) {\n      winSub.textContent = \"five jobs in parallel — and every one came back with its story\";\n    }\n    winTimer = setTimeout(() => {\n      closeTerminal(false);\n      tutorialEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n      winEl?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    }, 2600);\n  }\n  function submitCommand() {\n    const n = normalizeCmd(buffer);\n    buffer = \"\";\n    if (!n) { renderTerminal(); return; }\n    if (/^entire (checkpoint|cp) list$/.test(n)) { showList(); return; }\n    flashTerminal(\"command not recognized — try:  entire checkpoint list\", false);\n    renderTerminal();\n  }\n\n  // ---------- input ----------\n  function onCanvasClick() {\n    if (!started || failed) return;\n    if (active && !fp.isLocked && !terminalOpen) fp.lock();\n  }\n  function onKeyDown(e) {\n    if (!active) return;\n\n    // Briefing is up — Enter/Space begins the run, nothing else.\n    if (!started) {\n      if (e.code === \"Enter\" || e.code === \"Space\") { startLevel(); e.preventDefault(); }\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // The clock ran out — only R (retry) does anything.\n    if (failed) {\n      if (e.code === \"KeyR\") { resetLevel(); e.preventDefault(); }\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // While the terminal is open, all keys feed it.\n    if (terminalOpen) {\n      if (e.code === \"Escape\") { closeTerminal(true); e.preventDefault(); return; }\n      if (termMode === \"review\") {\n        if (!explained && e.code === \"Enter\") { runExplain(); e.preventDefault(); return; }\n        if (explained && (e.key === \"y\" || e.key === \"Y\")) { addToShip(); e.preventDefault(); return; }\n        e.preventDefault();\n        return;\n      }\n      // console mode — type the list command (the finish line)\n      if (listShown) { e.preventDefault(); return; }   // only Esc closes the list\n      if (e.code === \"Enter\") { submitCommand(); e.preventDefault(); return; }\n      if (e.code === \"Backspace\") { buffer = buffer.slice(0, -1); renderTerminal(); e.preventDefault(); return; }\n      if (e.key && e.key.length === 1 && !e.ctrlKey && !e.metaKey && !e.altKey) {\n        buffer += e.key;\n        renderTerminal();\n        e.preventDefault();\n      }\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // Walking around.\n    if (e.code === \"KeyE\" && nearSite?.state === \"broken\") { assignDrone(nearSite); e.preventDefault(); return; }\n    if (e.code === \"Escape\" && fp.isLocked) fp.unlock();\n    if (e.code === \"KeyB\") onExit?.();\n  }\n  fp.controls.addEventListener(\"lock\", () => crosshair?.classList.remove(\"hidden\"));\n  fp.controls.addEventListener(\"unlock\", () => crosshair?.classList.add(\"hidden\"));\n\n  // ---------- HUD per-frame ----------\n  let nearSite = null;\n  let nearConsole = false;\n\n  function refreshHud() {\n    if (!started || terminalOpen) {\n      setPrompt(null);\n      hideControls();\n      return;\n    }\n    setControls(fp.isLocked);\n    if (!fp.isLocked) { setPrompt(\"Click to look around\"); return; }\n    if (listShown) { setPrompt(\"The fleet's work is done — press B to return to orbit\"); return; }\n    if (nearSite) {\n      if (nearSite.state === \"broken\") setPrompt(`${nearSite.sys.name} is down — press E to send a drone`);\n      else if (nearSite.state === \"working\") setPrompt(\"Drone at work — you're free, go assign another\");\n      else if (nearSite.state === \"added\") setPrompt(`${nearSite.sys.became} online — accepted into the ship`);\n      return;\n    }\n    if (allAdded()) { setPrompt(\"Head for the gold beam — run `entire checkpoint list`\"); return; }\n    if (sites.some((s) => s.state === \"sealed\")) {\n      setPrompt(\"An ice-blue beam means finished work — go review it\");\n      return;\n    }\n    if (sites.some((s) => s.state === \"broken\")) {\n      setPrompt(\"Amber beams are broken systems — send the drones (E)\");\n      return;\n    }\n    setPrompt(\"The fleet is working — wait for an ice-blue beam, then go review\");\n  }\n\n  // ---------- per-frame ----------\n  function update(dt, t) {\n    fp.update(dt);\n\n    // Level countdown — one clock for the whole run.\n    if (active && timerRunning && !failed && !listShown) {\n      timeLeft = Math.max(0, timeLeft - dt);\n      updateClock();\n      if (timeLeft <= 0) failLevel();\n    }\n    applyPanicSky();\n\n    // Drones fly, work, and head home.\n    for (let i = 0; i < drones.length; i++) {\n      const d = drones[i];\n      const beam = workBeams[i];\n      if (d.state === \"bay\") {\n        d.model.position.y = d.home.y + Math.sin((t + d.bobOff) * 2) * 0.15;\n        d.model.userData.ring.rotation.z += dt * 1.2;\n      } else if (d.state === \"flying\" || d.state === \"returning\") {\n        d.t += dt;\n        const k = Math.min(1, d.t / d.dur);\n        const ease = k * k * (3 - 2 * k);\n        d.model.position.lerpVectors(d.from, d.to, ease);\n        d.model.position.y += Math.sin(k * Math.PI) * 3;   // arc over the island\n        d.model.userData.ring.rotation.z += dt * 6;\n        if (k >= 1) {\n          if (d.state === \"flying\") d.state = \"working\";\n          else { d.state = \"bay\"; d.model.position.copy(d.home); }\n        }\n      } else if (d.state === \"working\") {\n        const s = d.site;\n        d.model.position.set(\n          s.anchor.position.x + Math.cos(t * 1.4) * 1.2,\n          s.anchor.position.y + 6.5 + Math.sin(t * 2.2) * 0.4,\n          s.anchor.position.z + Math.sin(t * 1.4) * 1.2\n        );\n        d.model.userData.ring.rotation.z += dt * 9;\n        beam.visible = true;\n        beam.position.set(d.model.position.x, d.model.position.y - 3.2, d.model.position.z);\n        beam.material.opacity = 0.35 + Math.sin(t * 11) * 0.2;\n\n        // The actual work happens whether you watch or not.\n        if (active && started && !failed) {\n          s.workT += dt;\n          if (s.workT >= WORK_TIME) {\n            sealSite(s);\n            beam.visible = false;\n            d.state = \"returning\";\n            d.site = null;\n            d.from.copy(d.model.position);\n            d.to.copy(d.home);\n            d.t = 0;\n            d.dur = Math.max(1, d.from.distanceTo(d.to) / DRONE_SPEED);\n          }\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Sites: status beams, warning blinks, seals, and live upgrades.\n    for (const s of sites) {\n      if (s.state === \"broken\") {\n        s.beam.material.color.setHex(0xff8a5c);\n        s.beam.material.opacity = 0.24 + Math.sin(t * 1.6 + s.idx) * 0.08;\n        s.beam.visible = true;\n        const warn = s.brokenModel.userData.warn;\n        if (warn) warn.material.emissiveIntensity = Math.sin(t * 5 + s.idx) > 0 ? 1.8 : 0.2;\n      } else if (s.state === \"working\") {\n        s.beam.material.color.setHex(0x6fe3ff);\n        s.beam.material.opacity = 0.4 + Math.sin(t * 6) * 0.18;\n        s.beam.visible = true;\n      } else if (s.state === \"sealed\") {\n        s.beam.material.color.setHex(0x8fe3ff);\n        s.beam.material.opacity = 0.42;\n        s.beam.visible = true;\n      } else {\n        s.beam.visible = false;       // added — the upgrade itself is the landmark\n        if (s.meltT > 0) {            // the checkpoint seal melts away\n          s.meltT = Math.max(0, s.meltT - dt);\n          s.ice.material.opacity = 0.62 * (s.meltT / MELT_DUR);\n          if (s.meltT <= 0) s.ice.visible = false;\n        }\n        // The improvised upgrades live a little.\n        const anim = s.upgradeModel.userData.anim;\n        if (anim?.type === \"ring\") {\n          anim.ring.rotation.y += dt * 0.8;\n          anim.ring.position.y = 3.4 + Math.sin(t * 1.6) * 0.2;\n        } else if (anim?.type === \"dome\") {\n          anim.field.rotation.y += dt * 0.25;\n          anim.orbit.rotation.z += dt * 0.6;\n        } else if (anim?.type === \"skid\") {\n          anim.pad.position.y = 1.6 + Math.sin(t * 1.8) * 0.25;\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Proximity: nearest site, and the ship console.\n    nearSite = null;\n    let nd = Infinity;\n    for (const s of sites) {\n      const d = Math.hypot(\n        camera.position.x - s.anchor.position.x,\n        camera.position.z - s.anchor.position.z\n      );\n      if (d < nd) { nd = d; if (d <= INTERACT_DIST) nearSite = s; }\n    }\n    nearConsole = Math.hypot(\n      camera.position.x - CONSOLE_POS.x, camera.position.z - CONSOLE_POS.z\n    ) <= CONSOLE_DIST;\n    consoleGlow.intensity = allAdded() && !listShown ? 8 + Math.sin(t * 4) * 3 : 5;\n\n    // Terminals open themselves at the right places, like Level 1.\n    if (active && started && !failed) {\n      if (dismissedSite && nearSite !== dismissedSite) dismissedSite = null;\n      if (consoleDismissed && !nearConsole) consoleDismissed = false;\n      if (!terminalOpen) {\n        if (nearSite?.state === \"sealed\" && nearSite !== dismissedSite) {\n          openTerminal(\"review\", nearSite);\n        } else if (nearConsole && allAdded() && !listShown && !consoleDismissed) {\n          openTerminal(\"console\", null);\n        }\n      } else if (termMode === \"review\" &&\n                 (nearSite !== reviewSite || reviewSite.state !== \"sealed\")) {\n        // walked away mid-review (ADD closes it itself)\n        if (nearSite !== reviewSite) closeTerminal(false);\n      } else if (termMode === \"console\" && !nearConsole && !listShown) {\n        closeTerminal(false);\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (active) refreshHud();\n  }\n\n  // ---------- lifecycle ----------\n  function enter() {\n    active = true;\n    fp.groundAt(0, terrain.radius * 0.75);\n    camera.lookAt(CONSOLE_POS.x, terrain.heightAt(CONSOLE_POS.x, CONSOLE_POS.z) + 5, CONSOLE_POS.z);\n    canvas.addEventListener(\"click\", onCanvasClick);\n    window.addEventListener(\"keydown\", onKeyDown);\n    dbHud?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    fpShared?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    setControls(false);\n    crosshair?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    updateSystems();\n    if (!started) {\n      showBriefing();\n    } else if (listShown) {\n      fp.attach();\n      winEl?.classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n    } else if (failed) {\n      resetLevel();                 // came back after a wipe → fresh run\n    } else {\n      fp.attach();\n      timerRunning = true;          // resume the level clock\n      showTutorial(\"The drones kept working while you were in orbit.\", 5000);\n    }\n    updateClock();\n    applyPanicSky();\n  }\n  function exit() {\n    active = false;\n    timerRunning = false;           // pause the clock while in orbit\n    closeTerminal(false);\n    clearTimeout(winTimer);\n    fp.unlock();\n    fp.detach();\n    canvas.removeEventListener(\"click\", onCanvasClick);\n    window.removeEventListener(\"keydown\", onKeyDown);\n    setPrompt(null);\n    hideControls();\n    tutorialEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    termEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    failEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    winEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    briefingEl?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    dbHud?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    fpShared?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n    crosshair?.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n  }\n  function resize() {\n    camera.aspect = window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight;\n    camera.updateProjectionMatrix();\n  }\n\n  return { scene, camera, update, enter, exit, resize };\n}\n"
}

  "old_string": "const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);\nconst requestedView = params.get(\"view\");\nconst jumpToLevel2 = requestedView === \"level2\" || params.get(\"level\") === \"2\";\n\n// ---------- views ----------\n// After Level 1's `entire checkpoint list`, the ship's full archive surfaces:\n// the orbit pin glitches and landing again enters Level 2 (\"The Archive\").\nlet level1Done = jumpToLevel2;\n\nconst planetView = createPlanetView(renderer, {\n  onIslandClick: () => switchTo(level1Done ? archiveView : islandView),\n});\nconst islandView = createIslandView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n  onComplete: () => { level1Done = true; },\n});\nconst archiveView = createArchiveView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n});\n\n// Default to the orbit view; ?view=island jumps straight to Level 1 and\n// ?view=level2 (or ?level=2) straight to Level 2 — handy for development.\nlet current = requestedView === \"island\" ? islandView\n  : jumpToLevel2 ? archiveView : planetView;\ncurrent.enter();",
  "new_string": "const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);\nconst requestedView = params.get(\"view\");\nconst jumpToLevel2 = requestedView === \"level2\" || params.get(\"level\") === \"2\";\n\n// ---------- views ----------\n// After Level 1's `entire checkpoint list`, the ship wakes its drone bay:\n// the orbit pin glitches and landing again enters Level 2 (\"The Drone Bay\").\n// The shelved search level (\"The Archive\") stays reachable at ?view=archive.\nlet level1Done = jumpToLevel2;\n\nconst planetView = createPlanetView(renderer, {\n  onIslandClick: () => switchTo(level1Done ? droneBayView : islandView),\n});\nconst islandView = createIslandView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n  onComplete: () => { level1Done = true; },\n});\nconst droneBayView = createDroneBayView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n});\nconst archiveView = createArchiveView(renderer, {\n  onExit: () => switchTo(planetView),\n});\n\n// Default to the orbit view; ?view=island jumps straight to Level 1,\n// ?view=level2 (or ?level=2) to Level 2, ?view=archive to the shelved\n// search level — handy for development.\nlet current = requestedView === \"island\" ? islandView\n  : requestedView === \"archive\" ? archiveView\n  : jumpToLevel2 ? droneBayView : planetView;\ncurrent.enter();"

Every\n              job they finish is banked under a checkpoint, and the ship won't\n              power anything it can't account for. That part is yours.\n            </p>\n            <div class=\"bf-mission\">\n              <div class=\"bf-mission-head\">YOUR MISSION</div>\n              <ol class=\"bf-steps\">\n                <li>Walk to a dark system (amber beam) and press <code>E</code> — a drone takes the job while you move on.</li>\n                <li>When a beam turns ice-blue, the work is sealed under a checkpoint. Walk up — <code>entire checkpoint explain</code> comes pre-filled. Press <code>Enter</code>.</li>\n                <li>Read what the drone actually did, then <strong>ADD TO SHIP</strong> (<code>Y</code>).</li>\n                <li>All five online → run <code>entire checkpoint list</code> at the ship terminal before <strong>0:00</strong>.</li>\n              </ol>\n            </div>\n            <div class=\"bf-controls\">\n              <div class=\"bf-mission-head\">CONTROLS</div>\n              <div class=\"bf-ctrl-grid\">\n                <span class=\"bf-ctrl\">\n                  <span class=\"bf-keys\"><span class=\"bf-key\">↑</span><span class=\"bf-key\">←</span><span class=\"bf-key\">↓</span><span class=\"bf-key\">→</span></span>\n                  move <span class=\"bf-dim\">(or WASD)</span>\n                </span>\n                <span class=\"bf-ctrl\">\n                  <span class=\"bf-key\">mouse</span> look around <span class=\"bf-dim\">(click to capture)</span>\n                </span>\n                <span class=\"bf-ctrl\">\n                  <span class=\"bf-key\">E</span> send a drone\n                </span>\n                <span class=\"bf-ctrl\">\n                  <span class=\"bf-key\">B</span> return to orbit\n                </span>\n              </div>\n            </div>\n            <p class=\"bf-warn\">You can watch one drone work, or run five at once. The clock has an opinion.</p>\n            <button id=\"db-briefing-start\" type=\"button\">WAKE THE DRONES</button>\n            <div class=\"bf-hint\">or press <span class=\"bf-key\">Enter</span></div>\n          </div>\n        </div>\n\n        <!-- Level countdown — one clock for the whole run -->\n        <div id=\"db-countdown\">\n          <span class=\"cd-label\">TIME</span>\n          <span id=\"db-countdown-time\">2:30</span>\n        </div>\n\n        <!-- Fleet status — five systems, four states -->\n        <div id=\"db-systems\">\n          <div class=\"db-sys-head\">SHIP SYSTEMS</div>\n          <div id=\"db-systems-rows\"></div>\n        </div>\n\n        <!-- The fleet's day is on the record -->\n        <div id=\"db-win\" class=\"hidden\">\n          <div class=\"lw-title\">FLEET WORK ACCEPTED</div>\n          <div class=\"lw-sub\" id=\"db-win-sub\"></div>\n          <div class=\"lw-reveal\">That's <code>entire checkpoint explain</code> — work you didn't watch is never a mystery.</div>\n          <div class=\"lf-hint\">press <span class=\"lf-key\">B</span> to return to orbit</div>\n        </div>\n\n        <!-- The clock ran out -->\n        <div id=\"db-fail\" class=\"hidden\">\n          <div class=\"lf-title\">SYSTEMS STILL DARK</div>\n          <div class=\"lf-sub\">The clock ran out before every system was reviewed and added. Five drones, one of you — use the fleet.</div>\n          <div class=\"lf-hint\">press <span class=\"lf-key\">R</span> to try again</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    </div>"
}

  "old_string": "/* All three memories made it home */\n#l2-win {",
  "new_string": "/* ---------- Level 2 HUD (\"The Drone Bay\") ---------- */\n\n/* Fleet status panel — five systems, four states */\n#db-systems {\n  position: absolute;\n  top: 136px;\n  left: 32px;\n  width: min(290px, calc(100vw - 64px));\n  padding: 12px 14px;\n  background: rgba(6, 11, 22, 0.78);\n  border: 1px solid rgba(111, 227, 255, 0.32);\n  border-radius: 9px;\n  backdrop-filter: blur(8px);\n}\n.db-sys-head {\n  font-size: 10px;\n  letter-spacing: 0.22em;\n  font-weight: 700;\n  color: var(--accent);\n  margin-bottom: 9px;\n}\n.db-sys {\n  display: flex;\n  align-items: baseline;\n  justify-content: space-between;\n  gap: 10px;\n  padding: 3px 0;\n  font-size: 11px;\n  letter-spacing: 0.04em;\n}\n.db-sys-name { color: var(--ink); opacity: 0.85; white-space: nowrap; }\n.db-sys-state {\n  font-size: 9.5px;\n  font-weight: 700;\n  letter-spacing: 0.14em;\n  white-space: nowrap;\n}\n.db-sys.is-broken .db-sys-state { color: #ff8a5c; }\n.db-sys.is-working .db-sys-state { color: var(--accent); animation: db-work-blink 1.1s ease-in-out infinite; }\n.db-sys.is-sealed .db-sys-state { color: #bfe9ff; }\n.db-sys.is-added .db-sys-state { color: #7cffb0; }\n.db-sys.is-broken .db-sys-name { opacity: 0.6; }\n@keyframes db-work-blink { 50% { opacity: 0.45; } }\n\n/* All three memories made it home */\n#l2-win,\n#db-win {"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/planetfall-seed-signalkit/PLAN.md",
  "old_string": "### The teaching, felt not told\nDelegation is cheap and parallel; review is the human bottleneck — and every\ncheckpoint carries the story of work you didn't watch. Arc: L1 — bank it.\nL2 — delegate & review it. Search/rewind/recap/dispatch reserved for later\nlevels, by which point the checkpoint history will have grown naturally.",
  "new_string": "### The upgrade table (drones don't repair — they IMPROVISE)\nThe transformation is what makes you *need* the explanation: you left a bent\ndish, you come back to a lance pointed at the sky — \"what did you DO?\" is the\nurge `explain` exists to satisfy.\n\n| System (broken) | Becomes | Explain card flavor |\n|---|---|---|\n| Ignition coils (charred leaning stack) | **Plasma Ring** — floating gold torus | \"coils beyond saving — rebuilt as a plasma ring from salvaged hull plate · 2 attempts\" |\n| Nav core (cracked dark gyro) | **Star Dome** — projected starfield + orbiting ring | \"core unrecoverable — remapped from scratch; starfield calibrated to the lavender belt\" |\n| Long-range antenna (dish face-down in the dirt) | **Signal Spire** — lance firing a beam to orbit | \"dish unsalvageable — respun the mast into a spire · 3 attempts · the first two fell over\" |\n| Life support scrubbers (rusty vent box) | **Garden Pod** — glass dome, glowing plants | \"filters dead — replaced with a living filter; two vines scavenged from the crash\" |\n| Landing struts (collapsed leg) | **Grav Skid** — hover pad bobbing over a glow ring | \"strut seized solid — swapped for grav skids; technically the ship floats now\" |\n\nVisual grammar (status readable from across the island via beam color):\namber beam = broken (blinking red warn bulb up close) → cyan pulsing beam +\nhovering drone + work beam = drone at work → ice-blue beam + L1-style ice\nseal + floating checkpoint id = finished, review me → no beam = accepted\n(the upgrade itself is the landmark; ice melts, animations wake up).\n\n### The teaching, felt not told\nDelegation is cheap and parallel; review is the human bottleneck — and every\ncheckpoint carries the story of work you didn't watch. Arc: L1 — bank it.\nL2 — delegate & review it. Search/rewind/recap/dispatch reserved for later\nlevels, by which point the checkpoint history will have grown naturally.\n\n### Implementation (2026-06-10)\nLives in `src/droneBayView.js`; HUD block `#db-hud` in `index.html`; styles\nshare L1's countdown/briefing/fail selectors. The shelved Archive level stays\nfully playable at `?view=archive`; `?view=level2` / `?level=2` and the\npost-L1 glitching pin now route to the Drone Bay. Specifics:\n- 5 sites + 5 parked drones by the console (bay row at z=50, heights\n  node-verified). Drones arc to the site (~26 u/s), hover-orbit while\n  working (`WORK_TIME` 18s), then fly home. Work continues while the player\n  is elsewhere — that's the point.\n- One 150s clock (`TOTAL_TIME`); serial play (~35s/system × 5) can't beat it,\n  the fleet can. Same panic-sky as L1.\n- Fleet status panel (top-left): BROKEN / DRONE AT WORK / READY TO REVIEW /\n  ONLINE per system.\n- Review terminal auto-opens at a sealed site, pre-filled with\n  `entire checkpoint explain <id>` — Enter runs it (no id typing), card rows\n  print, then a green **ADD TO SHIP** button (clickable; Y also works).\n- Finish line: console terminal accepts typed `entire checkpoint list`,\n  prints all five \"drone fix: X → Y\" rows, then the win screen.\n- Fail at 0:00 → R resets everything (sites, drones, clock)."
}

> AGENT

Done — **The Drone Bay is built** and the upgrade table is locked into PLAN.md. Build passes; no browser testing on my end, it's ready for you at `?view=level2` / `?level=2` (or finish Level 1 and click the glitching pin).

**What you'll see** (`src/droneBayView.js`):

- **Five dark systems** under amber beams across the island, each with a blinking red warn bulb up close: leaning coil stack, cracked nav gyro, dish face-down in the dirt, rusty vent box, collapsed strut. **Five drones parked in a row** by the gold-glow console.
- **E at a broken site** → a drone arcs over from the bay, hover-orbits the site with a flickering work beam for 18 seconds, and the site's beam turns cyan. You're free the moment you press E — the fleet works whether you watch or not.
- **When a drone finishes**, it builds the improvisation — Plasma Ring, Star Dome, Signal Spire, Garden Pod, Grav Skid — seals it in Level 1's ice with a floating checkpoint id, flies home, and the beam goes ice-blue: *review me*.
- **Walk up to a sealed site** → the terminal auto-opens **pre-filled** with `entire checkpoint explain a1c9e4f72b05` — you only press Enter. The card prints (drone, system, what it did, the session flavor — "torque spec: vibes"), then a green **ADD TO SHIP** button appears (clickable, or press Y). The ice melts, the upgrade wakes up — the ring spins, the spire fires its beam to orbit, the skid hovers.
- **A fleet-status panel** top-left tracks all five: BROKEN / DRONE AT WORK / READY TO REVIEW / ONLINE — your air-traffic view while running parallel.
- **Finish line**: all five online → console terminal accepts `entire checkpoint list`, prints the five "drone fix: ignition coils → Plasma Ring" rows with the flash *"5 checkpoints · 5 upgrades · 1 crew member — look how much got done without you"*, then the win screen.
- **One 2:30 clock.** Serial babysitting (~35s per system) mathematically can't finish; the fleet can. Timeout → "SYSTEMS STILL DARK", R to retry.

The Archive level wasn't deleted — it's parked at `?view=archive`, and PLAN.md records it as a shelved future search level. The two tuning knobs for your playtest are at the top of `droneBayView.js`: `TOTAL_TIME` (150) and `WORK_TIME` (18).

we need an overhead birds eye view option sometimes so i can navigate things easier

> AGENT

Good call — especially for the Drone Bay, where you're juggling five sites at once. I'll add a toggleable bird's-eye view (press **M**) to all three island levels: fixed camera high above the island, a gold arrow marker showing where you are and which way you're facing, beams still color-coded so the map doubles as a status board. You can keep walking with the arrow keys while overhead.

First a shared helper, plus a small change to the walker so you can move while the map is up:

id: toolu_01PoVQuLA8pcKif1DaNPyr44
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/planetfall-seed-signalkit/src/overhead.js",
  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\n\n// Bird's-eye toggle for the island levels (press M): a fixed camera high\n// above the island, north up, plus a gold player marker (arrow = facing).\n// The status beams stay visible from above, so the map doubles as a status\n// board. The level keeps running — the clock doesn't care where you look.\n\nconst CAM_HEIGHT = 205;   // high enough that the whole island fits the frame\n\nexport function createOverhead(scene, terrain, playerCamera) {\n  const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(\n    55, window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight, 0.1, 2000\n  );\n  camera.position.set(0, CAM_HEIGHT, 0);\n  camera.up.set(0, 0, -1);\n  camera.lookAt(0, 0, 0);\n\n  // You-are-here marker: an arrowhead that points where you're facing,\n  // inside a soft ring so it reads at map scale.\n  const marker = new THREE.Group();\n  const arrow = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.ConeGeometry(2.0, 5.5, 4),\n    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: 0xffd27a, fog: false })\n  );\n  arrow.rotation.x = Math.PI / 2;          // cone +Y → +Z (points \"forward\")\n  arrow.position.y = 0.5;\n  const ring = new THREE.Mesh(\n    new THREE.RingGeometry(3.6, 4.4, 28),\n    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({\n      color: 0xffd27a, transparent: true, opacity: 0.55,\n      side: THREE.DoubleSide, fog: false,\n    })\n  );\n  ring.rotation.x = -Math.PI / 2;\n  marker.add(arrow, ring);\n  marker.visible = false;\n  scene.add(marker);\n\n  let on = false;\n  const dir = new THREE.Vector3();\n\n  function update(t) {\n    if (!on) return;\n    const px = playerCamera.position.x;\n    const pz = playerCamera.position.z;\n    marker.position.set(px, terrain.heightAt(px, pz) + 4, pz);\n    playerCamera.getWorldDirection(dir);\n    marker.rotation.y = Math.atan2(dir.x, dir.z);\n    ring.scale.setScalar(1 + Math.sin(t * 3) * 0.12);  // gentle pulse\n  }\n  function set(v) {\n    on = v;\n    marker.visible = v;\n  }\n  function resize() {\n    camera.aspect = window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight;\n    camera.updateProjectionMatrix();\n  }\n\n  return { camera, update, set, resize, get on() { return on; } };\n}\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Done — press **M** in any island level to toggle a bird's-eye view. Build passes; ready for your playtest.

How it works:

- **M toggles** a fixed camera high above the island (whole island in frame, north up) in all three levels — Level 1, the Drone Bay, and the shelved Archive. M again (or reaching a terminal) drops you back to first person.
- **A gold pulsing arrow** marks where you are and which way you're facing.
- **You can keep walking while overhead** — arrow keys still move you (I added an `alwaysMove` flag to the walker so the map doesn't require pointer lock), so you can steer yourself toward a beam from above and the terminal auto-opens when you arrive.
- **The beams make it a status board**, which is where it really pays off in the Drone Bay: amber = broken, cyan = drone working, ice-blue = ready to review — all five visible at once, matching the fleet panel. Fog is temporarily pushed back while the map is up so the island doesn't wash out.
- The clock keeps running while you're overhead — checking the map costs time like everything else.
- It auto-exits in every situation where it shouldn't linger: opening a terminal, failing, leaving to orbit. The M key is listed in all three briefings and the on-screen controls panel.

Implementation is one shared module (`src/overhead.js`, ~60 lines) wired into all three views identically, plus a 3-line change to `firstPerson.js`.

> AGENT

Fixed — the bird's-eye view now stays up when a terminal opens. Walk (or map-navigate) to a sealed site and the pre-filled `entire checkpoint explain` opens over the map; you review and ADD TO SHIP while still watching the other beams from above. Same in Level 1 and the Archive.

The map still exits only where it genuinely has to: pressing M again, failing the level, or leaving to orbit.

One note: while a terminal is open, every key feeds the terminal, so M won't toggle the map mid-terminal (in Level 1 you might legitimately type an "m"). If you're on the ground when a terminal auto-opens and want the map, it's Esc → M → walk back in. If that round-trip annoys you in the playtest, I can make M toggle the map inside the terminals that don't take typed input (the Drone Bay's review terminal only listens for Enter/Y/Esc).

> DEVELOPER

i cannot find the gold beam at the end ..where am i supposed to be going..maybe theres a better place..lets brainstorm

> AGENT

Found the root cause first: that's partly a bug on my end — the prompts say "head for the gold beam," but I never actually gave the Drone Bay console a beam. It only has a soft gold *glow* (a point light), which is invisible from across the island. The Archive level had a real sky-high beam at its console; the Drone Bay doesn't. So you were hunting for something that isn't there. Sorry about that.

But your instinct that there might be a *better place* is worth taking seriously. Three options:

**1. Just add the missing gold beam.** Console gets a tall warm beam that starts pulsing hard once all five systems are accepted. Smallest change, keeps the "home base bookend" — the level starts at the bay and ends at the bay. With the new bird's-eye map it'd be trivially findable.

**2. No travel at all — finish in the terminal you're already in.** When you press ADD TO SHIP on the *fifth* system, the terminal doesn't close. It just says: *"That's all five — close out the day"* and waits for you to type `entire checkpoint list` right there. Zero hunting, zero walking, and it mirrors real CLI flow — you don't walk anywhere to run `list`, it's just the next command at the same prompt. The win lands while your momentum is still up.

**3. The fleet shows you home.** After the fifth accept, all five drones launch and fly into formation above the console, forming a spinning gold column of drones — a "come here" beacon made of the workers themselves. Most charming, most work, and it gives the level a money-shot ending: you walk home under the thing your fleet built.

My honest ranking: **2** for game feel — after five review round-trips, one more fetch-quest to a terminal is friction, not tension, and "the next command happens at the prompt you're already at" is also the truest to the real CLI. Option **3** layered on top later if we want spectacle (the drones can still fly home behind you while the win screen shows).

Want me to do 2? Or 2 + the beam from 1 as a fallback landmark anyway (it costs nothing and fixes the lying prompt)?

		RunE: func(cmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
			var (

			if shouldRunDispatchWizard(cmd.Flags().NFlag(), isTerminalStdin(os.Stdin), interactive.IsTerminalWriter(cmd.OutOrStdout())) {

				return err

> AGENT

Done — the gold-beam hunt is gone and `entire dispatch` is the finish line. Build passes, ready to playtest.

What changed:

- **The fifth ADD TO SHIP no longer closes the terminal.** It flashes "all five systems online" and rolls straight into a report prompt: *"five jobs accepted — send the day's report"* → you type `entire dispatch` right where you're standing. No walking, no finding anything.
- **The dispatch output is the payoff**: a little report prints in the terminal — `DISPATCH · drone bay — day report`, the five upgrades with their checkpoint ids, *"filed from 5 checkpoints · crew: 1 human, 5 drones"* — then the win screen ("DISPATCH SENT") after a beat. The report writing itself from the checkpoints IS the value demo, matching the real command's purpose ("Generate a dispatch summarizing recent agent work").
- **`entire checkpoint list` still works at that prompt** as an L1 callback — it prints the raw log and nudges: *"now turn it into the day's report: entire dispatch."*
- **If you Esc or wander off** before dispatching, walking up to *any* online system (or the bay console) reopens the report prompt — so there's no single spot to relocate, every glowing upgrade is a valid place to file from.
- Briefing step 4 and the win screen copy updated; the misleading "gold beam" prompt is gone.

PLAN.md records the change and the reason (your playtest: a final fetch-quest was friction, and dispatch is the truer end-of-day command — explain stays the level's one taught mechanic, dispatch is the ceremonial closer like `list` was in L1).

One design note for your run-through: dispatch is now planted in the game twice — the Signal Beacon debris lore in orbit already says it's "broadcasting a dispatch into the dark." Nice accidental continuity.

> DEVELOPER

 # Planetfall
 
-A stranded astronaut whose ship's AI has lost its memory. To escape, you
-explore a 3D planet, find debris, and **recover lost context** — the same act
-that real [Entire](https://docs.entire.io) commands perform on a codebase
-(`recap`, `checkpoint`, `activity`, `dispatch`).
+Planetfall is a 3D browser game about a stranded astronaut whose ship AI has
+lost its memory. To get home, the player restores scattered ship memories while
+learning the real [Entire](https://docs.entire.io) checkpoint workflow:
 
-This is the **3D world prototype**: an explorable, realistically textured
-planet with clickable debris that seed the future context-recovery challenges.
-The challenge mechanics and win/lose flow come next.
+```text
+git add -> git commit -> link the commit to an Entire checkpoint
+```
+
+The current build is Level 1, **First Memories**. It teaches the difference
+between a commit and a checkpoint through a simple game loop: recover a memory,
+freeze it in ice with `git commit`, then bank it into the ship's memory by
+accepting Entire's checkpoint link.
 
 ## Run
 
 ```bash
 npm install
-npm run dev      # http://localhost:5173
+npm run dev
+```
+
+Open <http://localhost:5173>.
+
+Useful commands:
+
+```bash
```

> DEVELOPER

how would you summarize the two levels that we currently have

> AGENT

**Level 1 — First Memories** (the save habit). You crash-land and the ship's memory is scattered across the island as three glowing fragments. You walk to each one and work the real workflow at the terminal: `git add` stages it, `git commit` freezes it in ice, and when Entire offers, you press **y** to link a checkpoint — that's what actually banks it into the ship's memory, and the lesson lives in that distinction: a commit just freezes the change; the checkpoint is what makes the moment recoverable. All three banked, you run `entire checkpoint list` as the finish line — all under one 45-second clock with a sky that turns blood-red as it runs out.

**Level 2 — The Drone Bay** (the trust-and-review habit). The restored ship wakes its drone bay, but five systems are still dark — too many to fix yourself. You press E at a broken site and a drone takes the job while you move on; the single clock makes running all five in parallel the obviously rational choice. Drones *improvise* — your bent dish comes back as a signal spire, your dead scrubbers as a garden pod — each sealed under a checkpoint you didn't watch happen. That's what makes the level's command land: walking up opens a pre-filled `entire checkpoint explain`, you press Enter, read what the drone actually did, and hit **ADD TO SHIP**. The fifth accept rolls straight into the finale at the same prompt: `entire dispatch`, and the day's report writes itself from the five checkpoints.

The arc between them is the real arc of using Entire: **Level 1 teaches putting moments in** (saving is cheap, bank everything), **Level 2 teaches living with delegation** (agents work in parallel, the human is the review loop, and every checkpoint carries the story of work you never saw). One new mechanic per level — y/n checkpoint linking in L1, explain in L2 — with each finale being a ceremonial command that shows off what the banked history can do (`list`, then `dispatch`).

There's also a third level built but shelved: **The Archive** (`?view=archive`), where the ship asks for memories by keyword and `entire checkpoint search` lights the matching block across a field of ~21. It's waiting for a later slot, once the player's checkpoint history has grown enough that search feels necessary rather than staged.

> DEVELOPER

for level 2 does it teach delegate review accept..i feel like it's more showing you can have multiple agents working in parallel..see what work they did..and then get a summary in the end

> AGENT

You're right, and your version is the more honest description of what the player actually experiences. "Delegate → review → accept" was me imposing a workflow-diagram frame on it. Looking at what the level *actually does*:

- **Delegation isn't taught** — pressing E is one button with zero skill or command attached. What the level really shows is the *spectacle* of it: five agents working at once while you stand on a hill. That's a demonstration ("look what's possible"), not a lesson.
- **"Accept" isn't a lesson either** — ADD TO SHIP is just a button. Its real job is mechanical: it makes the explanation non-skippable. The gate exists so the reading happens.
- The two things the player genuinely *does* learn are the commands: **`entire checkpoint explain`** — see what any agent did while you weren't watching — and **`entire dispatch`** — get the whole day summarized at the end.

So the accurate summary is exactly yours: **multiple agents can work for you in parallel; you can see what each one did; and you can get a summary of all of it at the end.** Which maps one-to-one onto the product pitch, honestly — it's "here's what working with agents looks like when everything they do is checkpointed," with explain and dispatch as the two commands that make that world navigable.

I've updated the "teaching" section in PLAN.md and my notes to use that framing, so we don't drift back to the workflow-jargon version when we design Level 3.

> AGENT

No changes — just brainstorming. I checked each candidate against the real CLI so we're working from what they actually do:

- **`entire search`** — "Search checkpoints using semantic and keyword matching." Real, day-to-day, and we already own a shelved level for it.
- **`entire recap`** — "Summarize recent checkpoint activity." It's basically dispatch's sibling — L2 already plays the "summary at the end" beat, so a recap level would feel like a rerun. (We also rejected a recap design once before.)
- **`entire review`** — "Run configured review skills against the current branch." Tempting, but it's a hidden, experimental Labs command with interactive pickers and settings. Teaching it in the game means committing to something the team is still actively reshaping.
- **`entire attach`** — "Attach an existing agent session that wasn't captured by hooks." That's repair plumbing for when capture failed — no day-to-day story there.

So my honest read: **search is the one**, and the timing is finally right. When we shelved the Archive, the note we left ourselves was "reuse later when history has grown." That was the real problem with it — searching a pile of fake strangers' checkpoints on day one had no stakes. By Level 3 the player has *their own* history: five salvaged parts from Level 1, five drone upgrades from Level 2. Search only becomes valuable once there's a past worth digging through — which is also exactly the day-to-day truth of the command.

And it gives us the ending. The arc writes itself:

**Level 3: Launch Day.** The ship is repaired and upgraded — time to leave the planet. But the launch computer won't fire until it verifies the work that got you here. It asks in plain language, the way you'd actually half-remember your own work: *"something about ignition…"*, *"the one that turned into a garden…"*. You type `entire search ignition`, the matching checkpoint's beam lights up across the island, you go to it and confirm. Five verifications → engines light → takeoff cinematic → game ends with the full story of your history on screen. The thing you've been quietly building since the first `checkpoint` in Level 1 *is* the ending.

What makes me like it beyond the story:

- **One new command**, and it's the ceremonial-closer pattern inverted: in L1/L2 the closer command (list/dispatch) was the reward; here search is the *mechanic* and liftoff is the reward.
- **We've already built most of it.** archiveView.js has the search terminal, beam-on-match logic, seeded block placement. We'd reskin it from "mysterious archive" to "launch verification," swap fake history for the player's L1+L2 checkpoint ids, and add the takeoff finale.
- **No id typing, no deduction puzzles** — the clue tells you roughly what to search, search does the finding, you just walk to the light. Same complexity level as L2's explain flow.

The open question I'd want your gut on: does verification happen at the lit checkpoint (walk to each beam, like L1's collecting) or does each successful search just check a box on the launch console (less running around, but less island)? I'd lean walk-to-the-beam — it makes search feel like a flashlight over your own past, and the bird's-eye map we just added earns its keep.

![skills cover](assets/gh-repo-cover.png)

# skills

Bring your Entire context to your agents with cross-agent skills.

## Why This Exists

The [Entire CLI](https://github.com/entireio/cli) captures the context behind your
code changes: prompts, transcripts,
[Checkpoints](https://docs.entire.io/cli/checkpoints), and the decisions that led
to each change, alongside your git history. This repository packages
agent-invokable workflows that teach coding agents how to use that context across
development environments.

Instead of looking up Entire commands yourself, you can ask in plain language and
let your agent search prior work, inspect provenance, hand off session state, or
turn repeated workflows into new skills.

## Install

Install every skill with the [skills](https://skills.sh/) CLI:

```bash
npx skills add https://github.com/entireio/skills --all
```

Install one skill:

```bash
npx skills add https://github.com/entireio/skills --skill search
```

See [Agent-Specific Installation](#agent-specific-installation) for setup
instructions by agent.

## Quick Start

> [!NOTE]
> These skills are most useful in codebases with real
> [Checkpoints](https://docs.entire.io/cli/checkpoints) and session history. The
> richer the history, the more context your agent has to work with; in a new or
> lightly captured repository, some workflows may have less to find or explain.

After installing, ask your agent for the workflow you want:

```text
search past work for rate limiting
```

```text
explain src/auth.ts
```

```text
what happened here: src/auth.ts:42-57
```

```text
hand off this session
```

--- explain
---
name: explain
description: Explains the intent behind source code by finding original session transcripts. Use explain with a function, file, or line of code to understand why it exists.
argument-hint: <function, file, or line>
---

# Explain Intent

--- recall
---
name: recall
description: "Use when the user describes a task and wants to know whether something similar has been done before, then turn the closest prior session into a task playbook. Triggers on phrases like \"have we done this before\", \"recall how we did X\", \"find similar work\", \"any precedent for\", \"has anyone solved\", \"is there a template for\", and \"how did we do this last time\""
---

# Entire Recall

Use `entire search` and `entire explain` to recall the closest prior session for a task and turn it into a playbook the user can act on. This is task-shaped, not list-shaped: the goal is "here's how to do your task" rather than "here are some checkpoints."
--- replay
---
name: replay
description: "Use when the user wants to step through a feature's checkpoints chronologically, pausing at each step to ask questions. Triggers on phrases like \"replay <feature>\", \"walk me through how X was built\", \"show me the journey of\", \"step me through how Y was implemented\", and \"replay the last week\""
---

# Entire Replay

Use `entire search` and `entire explain` to sequence checkpoints chronologically and walk through them step by step, pausing for questions at each step. The pause-and-ask interaction is the core feature — do not dump all steps at once.
--- review
---
name: review
description: >
  Review code changes on the current branch using checkpoint transcript context
  to understand developer intent before auditing the diff. Use when the user
  asks for a code review, wants to review branch changes, or asks to audit
  recent work before merging.
---
--- search
---
name: search
description: Use when the user wants to find prior work, checkpoints, or agent conversations by topic, repo, branch, author, or recent time window
---

# Search Checkpoints

Use `entire search` to find relevant checkpoints before guessing from memory.
--- session-crosslink
---
name: session-crosslink
description: Use when an agent session ran outside the repo whose commits should record it — e.g. launched from a higher-level folder, a non-Entire repo, or one repo but editing another — to attach the session to each affected Entire-enabled repo's HEAD commit.
---

# Session Crosslink

## Response Format
--- session-handoff
---
name: session-handoff
description: Use when the user wants to continue work from one agent in another agent, inspect recent sessions, or summarize a saved session or checkpoint for handoff
---

# Hand-Off Session

## Response Format
--- session-to-skill
---
name: session-to-skill
description: Use when the user wants to turn one or more Entire-tracked sessions, checkpoints, or repeated agent workflows into a reusable agent skill.
---

# Session To Skill

Use this skill to help the user turn Entire session history into a focused skill draft.
--- teach
---
name: teach
description: "Use when a developer wants a topic-focused guided lesson built from canonical checkpoints, not a whole-repo overview. Triggers on phrases like \"teach me <topic>\", \"teach me how this repo handles\", \"how does <topic> work in this repo\", \"give me a lesson on\", \"school me on\", and \"I need to learn about\""
---

# Entire Teach

Use `entire search` and `entire explain` to pick 3-5 canonical checkpoints for a topic and teach the user as a guided lesson. Output is a structured lesson with a mental model and takeaways, not a list of checkpoints.
--- using-entire
---
name: using-entire
description: >
  Use for any codebase exploration or understanding task — reads historical
  intent from Entire checkpoints instead of guessing. Orchestrates other Entire
  skills to give the agent provenance-backed answers about code.
---

--- what-happened
---
name: what-happened
description: >
  Explain why code looks the way it does by tracing the latest change for a file
  range or pasted snippet through `git blame` and deduplicated `entire explain`
  lookups. Use when the user asks what happened, says "tell me why" about a code
  block, is confused about a section of code, asks "wtf is going on", "why is
  this like this", "why was this changed", or wants provenance for a specific
```

> AGENT

Oh, this is good — and it actually changes the shape of the finale. I read through the repo: it packages 11 agent-invokable skills (search, explain, recall, replay, review, teach, what-happened, session-handoff…) and the pitch is exactly the next rung of the ladder: *"Instead of looking up Entire commands yourself, you can ask in plain language and let your agent search prior work."* Installed via `npx skills add https://github.com/entireio/skills`.

So the product's real arc is:

1. **Capture** your work (checkpoint) — Level 1
2. **Agents in parallel**, see what each did, get a summary — Level 2
3. **Your history becomes ambient context** — you stop typing commands and just *ask*, and agents use everything you captured — that's the skills layer

Which suggests two shapes for Launch Day:

**Shape A — search stays raw.** The level I pitched before: launch computer gives plain-language clues, you type `entire search ignition`, beams light up, you verify, takeoff. The skills repo gets a plug on the win screen ("in real life, your agent can run these for you — `npx skills add …`").

**Shape B — the ship's computer is an agent.** Same level, but the twist *is* the lesson: for the first time in the game, you don't type an `entire` command. You ask the launch computer in plain language — "what happened to the antenna?" — and you *watch* it translate: the terminal prints `> entire search antenna` → results → beam lights up. You've spent two levels learning these commands by hand, so when the agent runs them, you know exactly what it's doing. Final screen: "Your agents can do this in your real repos too" + the skills install command. It still teaches `entire search` (you see it run every time), and it's the only honest ending for a game about agentic workflows: the player graduates from operator to delegator.

Mechanically B is barely harder than A — we keyword-match the player's free-text against each system's terms (we already do keyword matching in the shelved archiveView), and the "agent translating" is just printing the command before the results. Everything else (beams, verification walk, takeoff finale, the player's own L1+L2 checkpoint history) is identical between the two.

My gut says B — Shape A teaches a fourth command; Shape B teaches what the whole game was *for*. But B's free-text input needs to fail gracefully (what happens when someone types something we don't match?), and that's the one design problem A doesn't have. Worth thinking about whether a fuzzy "the computer didn't find anything for that — try describing the system" feels fine or feels fake.

No changes made — where does your gut land?

> DEVELOPER

eh

> AGENT

Fair — let me drop the launch-verification frame for a second, because I think that's the weak part: it's box-checking. There's no *problem*, just admin with beams.

The day-to-day moment that actually makes people reach for search is different: **something's wrong, and the answer is buried in work you (or an agent) did days ago.** That's a mystery, not a checklist.

And we already reserved the perfect setup for it — the "one drone botched it" twist we deliberately kept out of Level 2. As Level 3:

**Liftoff goes wrong.** You launch, something shudders, you're forced back down. One of the five drone upgrades is the culprit — but the level doesn't tell you which. Your only tool is your own history: search what was done to the suspect systems, read what each drone actually did, and find the one that cut a corner. Fix it (or have a drone redo it), launch clean, roll credits.

That makes search feel like what it is in real life — not a feature you're being taught, but the thing you grab when you're confused and the history has the answer. And it lands the story: the reason you've been checkpointing everything since Level 1 is for exactly this moment.

If that's also an eh — tell me what's off about the whole direction and we'll step back further. Is it the search-centric premise, the Launch Day frame, or just nothing's clicked yet?

> DEVELOPER

keep trying ideas with skills..maybe uncovering a mystery

> AGENT

Okay — skills + mystery. The skill names themselves are basically a detective kit: `what-happened` literally triggers on *"what happened here?"* and *"wtf is going on"*; `replay` is *"walk me through how this was built"*; `recall` is *"have we done this before?"*. Here's the direction that clicks for me:

**Level 3: The Other Ship.**

While prepping for launch, you pick up a signal from across the planet — another crashed ship. You fly/walk over and it's dead. Crew gone. No bodies, no note. But its black box survived: the crew's Entire history. Every checkpoint, every transcript, every decision they made in their final days.

The level is walking the wreck and interrogating the past. You find a scorched panel, a sealed door, an empty cryopod, a half-finished repair — and at each one the terminal pre-fills a plain-language question: **"what happened here?"** You hit Enter and *watch* the ship's computer work: it visibly runs `entire search thermal` → `entire explain 8b47…` underneath, then hands you a story card from the dead crew's transcripts. Piece by piece you reconstruct their last days — and the mystery resolves into something that matters to *you*: they made the same repair your drones just made, with the same flaw. Their history is the warning that saves your launch. You fix your ship, take off, credits.

Why I think this is the finale:

- **It's the real #1 use case, dramatized.** L1–L2 you *made* history. L3 you inherit someone else's — which is exactly the day-to-day problem the skills repo exists for: understanding work you weren't there for. Their README even warns skills shine in repos with rich history; the dead ship is a repo with rich history.
- **The new thing isn't a command — it's that you stop typing commands.** You ask in plain language, and because you spent two levels running `search` and `explain` by hand, you understand everything the agent does on your behalf. Graduation, not a fourth lesson.
- **Zero typing friction.** Pre-filled questions at each prop, same as L2's pre-filled explain — your verb is curiosity, not spelling. No free-text matching problem.
- **It redeems the Archive instinct.** "Searching strangers' history" felt like too much as an opener; as a finale it's the whole point, and now it has stakes and a body count.

Variants on the same skeleton, if the dead-crew tone feels heavy: the other ship is *your* ship's past (it crashed before, previous owner abandoned it — you've been rebuilding a haunted house); or there's no second ship and the mystery is buried in your own island (sealed compartment your drones never touched, older history inside).

The end screen writes itself: "Your agents can do this in any repo — `npx skills add https://github.com/entireio/skills`."

No changes made. Does the mystery-as-inherited-history shape feel right, or keep going?
